{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8d15f068-678c-4327-8e53-5715ca5c75dc","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Araştırma turu (salt-web + salt-okuma repo; dosya DEĞİŞTİRME, git KOŞMA). GÖREV: https://www.quantconnect.com/strategies (Strategy Library / Strategy Explorer) kataloğunu WebFetch/WebSearch ile derinlemesine tara ve Meridian'ın EDGE-ENVANTERİNE eşle.\n\nMERIDIAN EDGE-ENVANTERİ (research/cards/*.yaml dosyalarından DOĞRULA — özet):\nYAŞAYAN (2): uzun-ufuk mega-cap trend (~3-ay tutuş, seçim-kaynaklı edge) · turnover ana-etkisi (üst-%20 dilim, @20 net +0,55%).\nÖLÇÜLMÜŞ-ARŞİV (yeniden önerilmez; ancak YENİ mekanizma-açısı varsa not düşülür): 52wh-proximity, volume-shock bantları, MAX-etkisi (yön ters), residual momentum (rawmom-örtüşme), vol-scaling overlay, pullback, VCP-decompose, SMA-200 rejim kapısı (\"vol düşürür parayı da düşürür\"), ToM, EAP, insider-CMP, short-interest, PEAD-klasik (kill-list), post-event in-play (taban-sürüklenmesi dersi), net-issuance, gross-profitability. RAF: koşullu-kısa (motor long-only).\nKİLİTLER: NLP/analist verisi, delist-bar.\nÖLÇÜM YASALARI: taban-fazlası zorunlu (ham-getiri yasak), aynı-gün-evren kıyası, kill/success ön-kayıtlı, K-cezası.\n\nİŞ:\n1. Katalog envanteri: kaç strateji, hangi kategoriler, her girdinin yapısı (makale+kod+backtest?), lisans/kullanım şartı.\n2. HER İLGİLİ stratejiyi şu üç kovaya ayır: (a) BİZDE ÖLÇÜLDÜ-ARŞİV (hangi kartla örtüşüyor — ad ver; QC yazımı bizim ölçmediğimiz bir MEKANİZMA-VARYANTI içeriyorsa onu ayrıca işaretle); (b) YENİ KART-ADAYI (bizim evren/ufkumuza [large-cap günlük, 10-20g] uyarlanabilir + veri gereksinimi FREE'de karşılanır — her biri için: tez tek cümle, veri gereksinimi, beklenen kill-riski, kaynak URL); (c) UYGULANAMAZ (neden: intraday/opsiyon/kripto/veri-kilidi vb.).\n3. Kovaların sayıları + (b) kovasının TAMAMI ayrıntılı; en umut-verici 5'i öncelik-sıralı gerekçeyle.\n4. Backtest sonuçlarına GÜVENME notu: QC yazımlarının performans iddiaları bizim yasalarımızdan geçmemiştir — (b) adayları yalnız TEZ taşır, sayı taşımaz.\n\nDÖNÜŞ: yapılandırılmış rapor (kova sayıları, (b) tam listesi URL'li, ilk-5 gerekçeli, katalog-yapısı notları)."},"uuid":"6d3629b6-a237-4cfd-8996-c19549c40cf8","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:43:20.931Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"49a90f5c-72e2-4d1c-a9cd-f0a4ddb84bff","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- enterprise-search:digest: Generate a daily or weekly digest of activity across all connected sources. Use when catching up after time away, starting the day and wanting a summary of mentions and action items, or reviewing a week's decisions and document updates grouped by project.\n- enterprise-search:knowledge-synthesis: Combines search results from multiple sources into coherent, deduplicated answers with source attribution. Handles confidence scoring based on freshness and authority, and summarizes large result sets effectively.\n- enterprise-search:search: Search across all connected sources in one query. Trigger with \"find that doc about...\", \"what did we decide on...\", \"where was the conversation about...\", or when looking for a decision, document, or discussion that could live in chat, email, cloud storage, or a project tracker.\n- enterprise-search:search-strategy: Query decomposition and multi-source search orchestration. Breaks natural language questions into targeted searches per source, translates queries into source-specific syntax, ranks results by relevance, and handles ambiguity and fallback strategies.\n- enterprise-search:source-management: Manages connected MCP sources for enterprise search. Detects available sources, guides users to connect new ones, handles source priority ordering, and manages rate limiting awareness.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:brand-guidelines: Applies Anthropic's official brand colors and typography to any sort of artifact that may benefit from having Anthropic's look-and-feel. Use it when brand colors or style guidelines, visual formatting, or company design standards apply.\n- anthropic-skills:canvas-design: Create beautiful visual art in .png and .pdf documents using design philosophy. You should use this skill when the user asks to create a poster, piece of art, design, or other static piece. Create original visual designs, never copying existing artists' work to avoid copyright violations.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:contract-reviewer: Review any contract and flag what matters before you sign. Use this skill when the user says \"review this contract\", \"should I sign this\", \"check this agreement\", \"contract review\", \"what does this contract say\", \"flag anything bad\", \"review my lease\", \"NDA review\", \"freelance contract\", or pastes a contract, agreement, terms, or legal document they need reviewed before signing.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-architecture-icons: Build HPE-branded architecture diagrams and use the official HPE technical icon library (273 icons × 8 color variants, extracted from the official HPE technical icon collection). Use this skill whenever the user wants an architecture diagram, network/infrastructure/topology drawing, solution schematic, or needs official HPE icons (server, storage, switch, firewall, cloud, backup...) for documents, slides, or HLDs. Trigger on \"architecture diagram\", \"mimari çizim\", \"mimari diyagram\", \"topoloji çizimi\", \"HPE icon\", \"teknik ikon\", \"network diagram\", \"altyapı şeması\", \"draw the solution\", \"çözümü çiz\", and whenever an HPE HLD/SOW needs a figure. Pairs with the hpe-word-branding skill (insert the rendered PNG/SVG into branded docs).\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-pptx-branding: Create HPE-branded PowerPoint presentations from the official HPE-PowerPoint-Standard-R9 templates (Light and Dark). Use this skill whenever the user wants an HPE deck, slides, presentation, pitch, executive summary deck, QBR, or wants to convert an HLD/SOW/document into slides. Trigger on \"HPE deck\", \"HPE sunum\", \"HPE slides\", \"sunum hazırla\", \"slayt\", \"executive summary deck\", \"yönetici sunumu\", \"pptx\", and any presentation task in an HPE context. Sibling of hpe-word-branding (same brand rules) and hpe-architecture-icons (diagrams/icons for slides).\n- anthropic-skills:hpe-word-branding: Apply HPE (Hewlett Packard Enterprise) corporate branding to Word documents (.docx). Use whenever the user wants to: create an HPE-branded Word doc from scratch or from Markdown, fix/rebrand/reformat an existing .docx to HPE brand standards, audit brand compliance, apply HPE fonts/colors/table styles, format RACI matrices, add a revision history, compare two versions ('what changed'), batch-brand a folder, convert/rebrand a PDF into an HPE Word document, or produce any professional Word doc following HPE guidelines (SOW, proposal, HLD, report, memo). Trigger on \"HPE branded\", \"HPE format\", \"make it HPE\", \"HPE Word doc\", \"HPE proposal\", \"HPE SOW\", \"HPE HLD\", \"rebrand this document\", \"convert this PDF to HPE\", and Turkish equivalents like \"HPE formatına çevir\", \"HPE şablonuna geçir\", \"HPE marka uyumu\", \"formatı HPE yap\", \"HPE formatında hazırla\", \"PDF'yi HPE formatına çevir\". Always use this skill even if the user just says \"fix the formatting\" / \"formatı düzelt\" on an HPE-related document.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:workflow-visualizer: Map any system or workflow as a beautiful interactive HTML diagram\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- impeccable:impeccable: Use when the user wants to design, redesign, shape, critique, audit, polish, clarify, distill, harden, optimize, adapt, animate, colorize, extract, or otherwise improve a frontend interface. Covers websites, landing pages, dashboards, product UI, app shells, components, forms, settings, onboarding, and empty states. Handles UX review, visual hierarchy, information architecture, cognitive load, accessibility, performance, responsive behavior, theming, anti-patterns, typography, fonts, spacing, layout, alignment, color, motion, micro-interactions, UX copy, error states, edge cases, i18n, and reusable design systems or tokens. Also use for bland designs that need to become bolder or more delightful, loud designs that should become quieter, live browser iteration on UI elements, or ambitious visual effects that should feel technically extraordinary. Not for backend-only or non-UI tasks.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. QuantConnect'in survivorship-bias-free ABD hisse verisi\n  (delist dahil) bu şerhi İLK KEZ ölçülebilir kılar: aynı turnover üst-dilim tasarımı delist-dahil\n  evrende yeniden ölçülür. Hüküm sorusu: sinyal delist-gerçeğinde ne kadarıyla sağ?\nexecution_note: >\n  ÖLÇÜM MOTORU DIŞARIDA KOŞAR (QC research ortamı, operatör hesabı — hesap/kimlik operatörde;\n  Rol-1/ajanlar hesap açamaz/kimlik giremez). Ajan yalnız KENDİNE-YETERLİ defter (notebook) üretir;\n  OPERATÖR yapıştırıp koşturur, çıktı JSON'unu repoya bırakır; hüküm Rol-1'de. Bu, \"ölçüm ajanı\n  karta dokunmaz\" disiplininin dış-motor uyarlamasıdır: defter EŞİKLERİ İÇERMEZ (yalnız sayı üretir).\nuniverse: qc_us_equity_delist_dahil — S&P500-benzeri large-cap süzgeci defterde TANIMLI ve\n  raporda beyanlı (dolar-hacim üst-N; birebir SPX üyeliği QC'de ayrı veri — varsa kullanılır,\n  yoksa süzgeç-vekili beyan edilir); pencere 2020-08→2026-07 (yerel ölçümle örtüşen 6 yıl)\nfeatures_asof: \"turnover21(t) = medyan21(hacim)/shares_outstanding(t) — EDG-016 tanımına en yakın QC karşılığı; sapma varsa (ör. QC split-ayarı) defter raporda beyan eder\"\nhorizon: \"kesit dilim: günlük gözlem; ileri getiri 10/20g; taban = aynı-gün evren ortalaması; 21g blok CI (defter kendi bootstrap'ını taşır — bağımlılıksız)\"\nparameter_grid:\n  katman: [qc_turnover_ust20_fazlasi]   # K+=1 (tek hücre — doğrulama, arama değil)\ncost_model: \"10bps + 20bps duyarlılık satırı (EDG-016 ile kıyaslanabilirlik)\"\nsuccess_metric: >\n  Delist-dahil evrende üst-%20 turnover diliminin @20 taban-fazlası anlamlı POZİTİF (CI 0-dışı)\n  → sinyal delist-gerçeğinde SAĞ; büyüklük EDG-016'nın +0,65 brüt/+0,55 net değeriyle yan yana\n  raporlanır (fark = survivorship-priminin ölçüsü).\nkill_criteria:\n  - \"fazla CI-0-içi ya da negatif → YAŞAYAN SİNYAL ŞÜPHEDE: hüküm otomatik kill DEĞİL — Rol-1+operatör\n     birlikte değerlendirir (evren/tanım sapmaları önce elenir; ikinci koşum hakkı tanım-eşitleme\n     içindir, eşik-esnetme değildir)\"\n  - \"defter evren/tanım sapmasını kapatamıyorsa (ör. shares_outstanding as-of QC'de yoksa) →\n     'ölçülemedi' beyanı, kart askıya (uydurma vekil YASAK)\"\nguards:\n  - \"pozitif kontrol defterin İÇİNDE: rvol20 @20 IC işareti/mertebesi (yerel çivi ≈0.064) QC evreninde\n     yeniden üretilir — üretilemezse defter DUR der ve nedenini basar\"\n  - \"defter deterministik (sabit tohum), tek dosya, dış bağımlılıksız; çıktı JSON şeması kartta\n     (sonuc_021.json: hücre/CI/n/evren-sayıları/PK/beyanlar)\"\n  - \"operatör koşum kanıtı: QC çıktısının TAM JSON'u research/olcumler/qc_dogrulama/'ya konur;\n     hüküm kanıtla aynı commit'te\"\nk_registry: {registered_at: \"2026-08-03T00:40Z\", trial_ids: [qc_dogrulama/turnover_ust20]}\nstatus: registered\nnotes: >\n  Operatör QC hesabını 2026-08-03'te açtı. Akış: (1) ajan defteri üretir →\n  research/qc_dogrulama/qc_defter_021.py + OPERATOR_TALIMATI.md; (2) operatör QC Research'te\n  yeni notebook'a yapıştırır, koşturur (~dk mertebesi), son hücrenin JSON çıktısını kopyalayıp\n  research/olcumler/qc_dogrulama/sonuc_021.json olarak kaydeder (ya da bana yapıştırır);\n  (3) hüküm Rol-1. Cite the source paper."},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2039,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5132,"cache_read_input_tokens":47126,"output_tokens":2903,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5132},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2039,"output_tokens":2903,"cache_read_input_tokens":47126,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5132,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":5132,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdejPi8bRF8fLiNG66phY","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"b3c1f53b-2a57-4853-8db0-0a8b71db24a1","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:44:54.983Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"6cd4db7b-f5bd-4d7a-9aac-295a6ecfb313","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01N5UCXJwGskUdPuSiAATfwr","type":"tool_result","content":"# 12 Month Cycle in Cross-Section of Stocks Returns Strategy\n\n## Signal Definition\nThe strategy exploits seasonal patterns by ranking stocks based on their performance during January of the previous year. It hypothesizes that stocks performing well in last year's January will repeat that performance in the current January.\n\n## Universe\n- **Securities**: All stocks from AMEX and NYSE exchanges\n- **Filtering**: Stocks must have fundamental data available\n- **Size constraint**: Top 30% by market cap (calculated using BasicAverageShares, BasicEPS, and PERatio)\n\n## Rebalance Frequency & Holding Period\nMonthly rebalancing occurs, with positions held throughout each month.\n\n## Ranking Rule (Precise Quote)\n\"Every month, stocks are grouped into ten portfolios with the equal number of stocks in each portfolio according to their performance in January one year ago.\"\n\nThe stocks are ranked by returns calculated as the difference between closing prices 365 days ago and 335 days ago (approximating the January period from the prior year).\n\n## Trading Logic\n- **Long**: Top decile (highest January performance one year ago)\n- **Short**: Bottom decile (lowest January performance one year ago)\n- **Weighting**: Equally weighted within each decile, sizing at 1/(portfolio_size×2) per position\n\n## Data Sources Required\n- Historical price data (365 and 335 days prior)\n- Fundamental data (market cap, BasicAverageShares, BasicEPS, PERatio)\n\n## Academic Reference\nThe strategy references Quantpedia's \"12 Month Cycle in Cross-Section of Stocks Returns\" research."}]},"uuid":"d412f1a3-afa2-4935-93dc-8e1529c0da60","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:44:59.594Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6cd4db7b-f5bd-4d7a-9aac-295a6ecfb313","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"d1cb5971-8d21-4fca-b11c-b97d242c3337","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VXsnMSMUBm37nvEnih8W6S","type":"tool_result","content":"# Seasonality Effect Strategy: Mechanism Summary\n\n## Academic Source\n**Paper:** \"Common Factors in Return Seasonalities\" (NBER Working Paper w20815)\n\n## Universe Selection\n- Top 100 liquid securities and ETFs ranked by dollar volume\n- Price filter: Greater than $5\n- Includes U.S. equities, commodities, and index assets\n\n## Signal Definition & Ranking Rule\nThe strategy calculates monthly returns for the identical calendar month from the previous year. Securities are ranked by these historical returns. According to the research, this approach generates \"an average monthly return of 1.88%.\"\n\n**Precise ranking mechanism:**\n- **Long positions:** Top performers by same-calendar-month prior-year returns\n- **Short positions:** Worst performers by same-calendar-month prior-year returns\n\n## Position Sizing\nEqual-weighted allocation across all long and short holdings\n\n## Rebalance Frequency\nMonthly, executed at month-end\n\n## Holding Period\nOne calendar month\n\n## Data Sources Required\n- Daily closing prices for historical return calculations\n- Dollar volume data for universe construction\n\n## Implementation Note\nThe backtest achieved a Sharpe ratio of 0.128 over 10 years, versus 0.773 for SPY, suggesting opportunities for enhancement through multi-year historical lookback periods or alternative universe construction criteria."}]},"uuid":"aeac0d5b-1b10-4647-8f5d-d120ffd48cbf","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:44:59.780Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d1cb5971-8d21-4fca-b11c-b97d242c3337","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"e94c83bd-d360-462f-911f-5a04df70005d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015PNpCtrEVPDohDBRW7kpyQ","type":"tool_result","content":"# Expected Idiosyncratic Skewness Strategy\n\n## Signal Definition\nThe strategy computes expected idiosyncratic skewness through a multi-step process:\n\n1. **Fama-French Regression**: Calculate daily residuals using the three-factor model to isolate idiosyncratic returns\n2. **Historical Moments**: Compute idiosyncratic volatility and skewness from monthly residuals\n3. **Cross-sectional Prediction**: Regress current-month skewness against lagged skewness and volatility, then use fitted parameters to forecast next-month expected skewness\n\nThe paper notes that \"lagged idiosyncratic volatility is a stronger predictor of skewness than lagged idiosyncratic skewness.\"\n\n## Universe & Rebalancing\n- **Universe**: 200 liquid stocks selected by daily volume, price, and fundamental data availability\n- **Rebalance Frequency**: Monthly\n- **Holding Period**: One month\n- **Position Construction**: Long the bottom 5% of expected skewness stocks, value-weighted\n\n## Performance\n\"The Sharpe ratio is 0.947 relative to S&P 500 (SPY) Sharpe ratio of 0.87 during the period of July 1, 2009 to July 30, 2019.\"\n\n## Data Sources\n- Kenneth French's Data Library (Fama-French factors)\n- QuantConnect's Dataset Market (prices, volume, market cap)\n\n## Academic Foundation\n**Boyer, Mitton, and Vorkink (2009)**, *The Review of Financial Studies*: Demonstrates that \"stocks with high idiosyncratic skewness have low expected returns\" due to investor preference heterogeneity."}]},"uuid":"45b3db39-19c4-4dbd-8288-72318482689f","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:02.125Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e94c83bd-d360-462f-911f-5a04df70005d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"b3c1f53b-2a57-4853-8db0-0a8b71db24a1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AaCA1ieYvwdjCoiX88Qkbz","type":"tool_result","content":"# Liquidity Effect in Stocks Strategy\n\n## Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Liquidity/Turnover Definition:**\nThe strategy measures liquidity via \"annual turnover which is the number of shares traded divided by the stock's outstanding shares.\" This metric is preferred over raw volume because it normalizes for market capitalization differences.\n\n**Universe:**\n- Coarse filter: stocks priced above $5, excluding ADRs, ETFs, and closed-end funds\n- Fine filter: market cap minimum of $10 million\n- Stocks are divided into quartiles by market capitalization, focusing on the lowest quartile\n- Within the smallest-cap quartile, stocks are split by turnover into 5th and 95th percentiles\n\n**Rebalance Frequency & Holding Period:**\n\"The portfolio is rebalanced once a year and stocks are weighted equally.\"\n\n**Direction of Effect:**\nThe strategy takes **long positions in low-turnover stocks** (5th percentile) and **short positions in high-turnover stocks** (95th percentile), indicating that illiquid small-cap stocks are expected to outperform liquid ones.\n\n## Source\nThe research cites **Quantpedia Premium - Liquidity Effect in Stocks** as its reference, though the original academic source is not explicitly named in this document."}]},"uuid":"fdac8a66-6f25-47bb-b308-4d548699384b","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:02.329Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b3c1f53b-2a57-4853-8db0-0a8b71db24a1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"8b317733-07cd-49d1-bd25-aefe9d9fbf6b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RLJvMYgF5ZuxkQgkc72Nvg","type":"tool_result","content":"# CAPM Alpha Ranking Strategy on Dow 30 Companies\n\n## Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Alpha Computation:**\nThe strategy employs linear regression to identify alpha (the intercept) by regressing individual stock returns against benchmark (SPY) returns. As stated in the document, \"the slope and intercept will be beta and alpha\" when regressing stock returns against benchmark returns.\n\n**Universe:**\nThe strategy focuses exclusively on the 30 components of the Dow Jones Industrial Average. The document notes \"the earliest start date of this algorithm is March 19th, 2015\" due to component stability.\n\n**Lookback Period:**\nHistorical data spans \"the past 21 trading days\" to calculate daily returns for the regression analysis.\n\n**Rebalance Frequency:**\nThe algorithm rebalances monthly, triggered on \"the first trading day of each month.\"\n\n**Holding Period:**\nAssets are held for one month until the next rebalancing occurs, based on the assumption that \"stocks which beat the market last month will continue to beat the market.\"\n\n**Long-Only vs. Long-Short:**\nThis is a long-only strategy. The algorithm \"liquidate[s] all our positions and purchase[s] the first 2 stocks\" ranked highest by alpha, investing \"100% in the each of the selected symbols.\"\n\n**Data Requirements:**\nDaily closing prices for Dow 30 constituents and the SPY benchmark index are required."}]},"uuid":"d417582c-a577-446a-9f06-489bd4bb2d63","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:19.642Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8b317733-07cd-49d1-bd25-aefe9d9fbf6b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"2622a104-3fe4-4290-81ad-f415890f7582","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QspmNVGFXfXAm2wcCaCfDf","type":"tool_result","content":"# Mean Reversion Statistical Arbitrage Strategy: Mechanism Overview\n\n## Strategy Components\n\n**Universe Selection:**\nThe algorithm filters equities with prices above $5, then selects the top 20 stocks by dollar-traded volume.\n\n**Signal Definition:**\nThe strategy identifies trading signals through z-scores of regression residuals. It targets stocks with z-scores below -1.5, indicating prices have deviated significantly below their mean-reverting trend. Portfolio weights are inversely proportional to the absolute z-score values.\n\n**Dimensionality Reduction:**\n\"PCA is a procedure that extracts uncorrelated components of a possibly-correlated set of observations.\" The algorithm reduces the feature space to 3 principal components derived from historical closing prices, minimizing market factor exposure.\n\n**Regression Model:**\nLinear regression (OLS) regresses individual stock prices against the three principal components, generating residuals that measure price deviation from expected values.\n\n**Rebalancing & Holding:**\nPortfolio rebalancing occurs every 30 days. The backtest period spans January 2010 through August 2019.\n\n**Performance Results:**\nThe strategy achieved approximately 6% annual returns with a maximum drawdown of ~49% over the nearly 10-year period.\n\n## Source\nThe strategy derives from the academic paper: \"Statistical Arbitrage in the U.S. Equities Market\" (referenced in the original research post)."}]},"uuid":"582d9b7b-ca2f-4199-b6a2-45b041bd3e15","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:20.541Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2622a104-3fe4-4290-81ad-f415890f7582","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"47708e70-74dd-4a14-9f40-3e6979920f28","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RWJ6cUqJhKUHprBTTCwauB","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum and State of Market Filters Strategy\n\n## Market-State Filter\nThe strategy employs a binary market regime filter based on the Wilshire 5000 Total Market Index's 12-month return. \"If the market's one-year return is positive, we define the state of the market as 'UP' otherwise the state is 'DOWN'.\" During UP regimes, momentum strategies are active; during DOWN regimes, all equity positions are liquidated and capital is allocated to long-term Treasury bonds (TLT ETF).\n\n## Momentum Definition\nMomentum is measured using the 6-month Momentum Percent (MOMP) indicator applied to individual stock prices. Stocks are ranked by this metric monthly.\n\n## Universe and Holding Period\n- **Universe**: All NYSE and NASDAQ stocks priced above $1 with fundamental data available\n- **Rebalancing**: Monthly\n- **Holding Period**: One month (rebalanced at month start)\n\n## Positions\n- **Long**: Top 20 stocks by 6-month MOMP (equally weighted at 0.5/20 each)\n- **Short**: Bottom 20 stocks by 6-month MOMP (equally weighted at -0.5/20 each)\n- **Active only during UP market regimes**\n\n## Data Requirements\n- Wilshire 5000 Total Market Index daily prices (from Federal Reserve/FRED dataset; note: discontinued)\n- Individual stock adjusted prices and fundamental data\n\n## Source Paper\nCooper, Gutierrez, and Hameed's \"Market States and Momentum\" (SSRN Abstract ID 299927) provides theoretical foundation for the 12-month market-state evaluation period."}]},"uuid":"4992d0b6-2c37-4c12-b546-9fdc15cde5b0","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:22.623Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"47708e70-74dd-4a14-9f40-3e6979920f28","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"4ef25d1c-7482-44ef-8b8e-711abc11ad27","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018CKg7Ese8FQhw2eLNHEQVz","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum-Volume Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Universe & Data:**\nThe strategy trades \"all stocks on NYSE and NASDAQ\" with fundamental data requirements. It uses the Rate of Change indicator over a 12-month lookback period to measure momentum.\n\n**Volume Integration:**\nTurnover serves as the volume metric, calculated as \"the ratio of the number of shares traded each day to the number of shares outstanding.\" This turnover measure helps identify investor interest and potential future price direction.\n\n**Stock Selection Process:**\nMonthly rebalancing divides eligible securities into momentum deciles based on prior 12-month returns. Within the top and bottom deciles, stocks are then ranked by turnover. The strategy constructs long positions from \"the highest turnover from the top momentum decile\" and short positions from \"the highest volume from the bottom momentum decile.\"\n\n**Position Sizing & Holding Period:**\nPortfolios are held for three months with rolling monthly rebalancing. Each month, the strategy adds one-third of the intended portfolio (equally weighted within long and short legs) and liquidates positions three months old.\n\n**Direction of Volume Tilt:**\nThe strategy favors **high-volume stocks** in both directions—selecting high-turnover names among strong momentum stocks for longs and high-turnover names among weak momentum stocks for shorts."}]},"uuid":"7d6a98da-a652-49e2-ac3c-a80a1e4288ce","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:23.615Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4ef25d1c-7482-44ef-8b8e-711abc11ad27","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"b246c00d-942e-4f80-b16d-5b4a3efca188","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CDdcEPiiNHgcVRhw4SWphx","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum-Reversal-Volatility Strategy Mechanism\n\n## Universe Selection\nThe strategy filters for NYSE, AMEX, and NASDAQ stocks priced above $5. It then divides this universe into two equal halves by market capitalization, selecting only the larger-cap stocks.\n\n## Volatility-Based Sorting\n\"Stocks are sorted into quintiles based on their realized volatility. Stocks in the top 20% highest volatility are further sorted into quintiles by their six-month realized returns.\"\n\n## Long/Short Construction\nWithin the highest volatility group, the strategy goes long the top quintile of performers and short the bottom quintile.\n\n## Lookback & Holding Period\nThe strategy employs a 6-month return calculation period with a 6-month holding period. One week prior to month-end is excluded \"to avoid biases due to microstructures.\"\n\n## Rebalance Frequency\nMonthly rebalancing occurs with staggered portfolio implementation. \"1/6 of the portfolio is rebalanced every month\" through a rolling 6-portfolio structure—each position held for six months while new selections enter monthly.\n\n## Data Requirements\n- 6-month warm-up period for historical prices\n- Daily price data for return and volatility calculations\n- Realized volatility annualized using \"the square root of the number of trading days in a year – in our case square root of 252\"\n\n## Source\nQuantpedia - Momentum and Reversal Combined with Volatility Effect in Stocks"}]},"uuid":"f999d742-d217-4950-ad31-17acc4dbea9d","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:43.657Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b246c00d-942e-4f80-b16d-5b4a3efca188","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"c1149a70-7dda-478a-b957-606f1f5b9fce","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VK7LQxQ6e9qPXQKCi5hVQB","type":"tool_result","content":"# Pre-Holiday Effect Strategy Mechanism\n\n## Holiday Selection\nThe strategy identifies **public holidays** within the next two trading days using the TradingCalendar class, specifically `TradingDayType.PUBLIC_HOLIDAY`. Weekend dates are excluded from the calculation.\n\n## Timing\nTrading occurs during the **two trading days preceding public holidays**. The algorithm checks daily if any holidays exist within the upcoming two-day window.\n\n## Asset & Universe\nThe strategy trades **SPDR S&P 500 ETF (SPY)** exclusively—a single-security approach rather than a broad universe.\n\n## Position Management\n- **Entry**: \"if not self.portfolio.invested and len(holidays)>0: self.set_holdings(\"SPY\", 1)\"\n- **Exit**: When no holidays remain within the two-day lookahead window, the position is liquidated\n\nThe holding period is **variable**, lasting only while pre-holiday conditions persist.\n\n## Data Requirements\n- Daily price data for SPY\n- Trading calendar with public holiday dates\n- Standard OHLCV (open, high, low, close, volume) data\n\n## Economic Rationale\nThe strategy exploits lower liquidity and reduced market participation before holidays, which historically creates \"market in-efficiency for short-term traders\" during these periods."}]},"uuid":"49479ea8-4475-4cac-bea8-768008bb464b","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:44.938Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c1149a70-7dda-478a-b957-606f1f5b9fce","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"3119d239-7a81-4b89-9c0b-2e85d50a00ca","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015SyGNR3Xti1vRWnzPmuvLG","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum Strategy for Small Portfolios\n\n## Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Universe:** All US-listed companies with fundamental data available, excluding the lowest 25% by market capitalization due to liquidity concerns.\n\n**Momentum Lookback:** 12 months of historical returns determine ranking.\n\n**Portfolio Size:** \"The investor goes long in the ten stocks with the highest performance and goes short in the ten stocks with the lowest performance.\"\n\n**Rebalancing & Holding:** Annual rebalancing with yearly holding periods.\n\n**Positioning:** The portfolio is equally weighted at 50% long (0.5/10 per stock) and 50% short (0.5/10 per stock).\n\n## What Makes It \"Small Portfolio\"\n\nThe research addresses retail investor constraints: \"Momentum strategy usually uses portfolios filled by thousands of stocks to compute the momentum factor return. This is not possible for small retail investors with small portfolios.\" By limiting to 20 stocks total (10 long/10 short), this approach adapts the momentum anomaly for individual investors unable to achieve institutional diversification levels.\n\n## Source\n\nQuantpedia - Momentum Effect in Stocks in Small Portfolios (referenced in the research publication)"}]},"uuid":"c6fe41b2-b6c3-4d6e-bfbd-0e2b82bdeead","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:45:46.685Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3119d239-7a81-4b89-9c0b-2e85d50a00ca","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"051c81a7-8176-46fa-b1e5-530e7febc879","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Y9Fc9PYVFeTj1CEz9Dv7Tk","type":"tool_result","content":"# Commodities Futures Trend Following: Signal, Exits & Sizing\n\n## Entry Signal\n\nThe strategy generates buy/sell signals monthly based on a momentum calculation:\n\n**Signal Formula:**\n```\nsignal = (current_price - exponential_moving_average) / volatility\n```\n\nA position is initiated when this signal is non-zero. The direction (long/short) depends on the sign: positive signals trigger buys, negative signals trigger sells.\n\n## Exit Rules\n\n**The research contains no explicit exit rules.** The strategy description provides no stops, trailing stops, time-based exits, or profit targets. The only mention regarding position management is a staff comment noting logic was \"added to liquidate old contracts when rollovers occur in order to avoid running out of margin\"—addressing operational constraints rather than systematic exit criteria.\n\n## Position Sizing\n\nPosition size uses **volatility-based scaling**:\n\n> \"buys or sells a quantity of σₙ⁻¹ of the underlying contract depending on the sign of sₙ\"\n\nMore specifically: **\"quantity = sign(signal) × momentum_value / absolute(volatility)\"**\n\nThis inverse-volatility approach means:\n- High volatility → smaller position size\n- Low volatility → larger position size\n\nThe algorithm then applies leverage (\"3x\") when executing orders: `quantity × leverage / contract_multiplier`\n\n**Critical Note:** Backtest results (Jan 2010–Jan 2020) showed poor performance with Sharpe ratio of -0.131, suggesting this simple trend signal no longer generates alpha in modern markets."}]},"uuid":"1a238463-2020-4501-8db5-6ed33ee39e7f","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:16.174Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"051c81a7-8176-46fa-b1e5-530e7febc879","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"5e42b247-6e9c-4bd1-97d5-c3aee3fd3541","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PmyrF5HBseaq1sxivRbVgg","type":"tool_result","content":"# Position Sizing in the Baltas-Kosowski TSMOM-CF Strategy\n\n## Core Sizing Formula\n\nThe primary weighting equation is:\n\n\"r_{t,t+1}^{TSMOM-CF} = \\\\frac{1}{N_t} \\\\sum_{i=1}^{N_t} X_t^i \\\\frac{\\\\sigma_{P,tgt}}{\\\\sigma_t^i} CF(\\\\bar{\\\\rho}_t)r_{t,t+1}^i\"\n\nEach position's weight combines three multiplicative components.\n\n## Three Components of Position Sizing\n\n**1. Trend Strength Signal (X_t^i)**\nThe TREND rule generates a continuous signal bounded between -1 and +1, derived from the t-statistic of 12-month daily log-returns. When the absolute t-statistic exceeds 1, \"the strategy puts 100% exposure to the asset.\" For weaker trends (t-stat between -1 and 1), exposure scales proportionally, allowing the signal to \"modulate...exposure to less than 100%.\"\n\n**2. Volatility Targeting (σ_{P,tgt} / σ_t^i)**\nThe strategy employs a portfolio target volatility of 12%. Individual position volatility is estimated using the Yang-Zhang method, which incorporates overnight gaps and intraday ranges. Each position is scaled inversely to its volatility: higher volatility assets receive proportionally smaller positions to maintain consistent portfolio risk.\n\n**3. Correlation Factor (CF)**\nComputed as \"CF(\\\\bar{\\\\rho}) = \\\\sqrt{\\\\frac{N}{1+(N-1)\\\\bar{\\\\rho}}}\", the correlation factor adjusts overall leverage dynamically based on average pairwise asset correlations calculated over the preceding three months. Higher correlation reduces leverage across all positions.\n\n## Rebalance Frequency & Exit Rules\n\nThe strategy rebalances **monthly**, triggered at month-end. The document specifies that positions are \"clipped\" at ±100% (\"np.clip(weight, -1, 1)\"), establishing implicit maximum exposure limits. No explicit stop-loss or exit mechanism is documented beyond portfolio rebalancing."}]},"uuid":"6cfc5a95-3e48-4d1c-9eed-0cebf3e02ccd","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:16.343Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5e42b247-6e9c-4bd1-97d5-c3aee3fd3541","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"908c2dba-3513-4c5b-a74a-defd0ea1a8fa","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"dd8d0370-5a27-49ac-af90-2a1901345b0c","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SzEQQymg1if8t8hsRAgbJs","type":"tool_result","content":"# Risk Management Overlay Analysis\n\n## Position Management Signal\n\nThe moving-average overlay operates as a **complete on/off switch** rather than a position-size modulator. As stated: \"if the current price of SOO is above the 200-day SMA, we hold SSO, and if SSO dips below our 200-day SMA, we liquidate our position.\"\n\n## Exit Rules\n\nPositions are exited through tactical rotation:\n- When SSO falls below its 200-day simple moving average, the strategy fully liquidates the leveraged equity position and rotates into \"short-term treasuries, which is done through SHY, an ETF that tracks 1-3 year U.S. Treasury Bonds\"\n- The inverse applies: holdings in treasuries are liquidated when SSO rises above the moving-average threshold to re-enter equities\n\n## Rebalance Frequency\n\nThe strategy employs a **200-day moving-average window** specifically to minimize turnover. The authors deliberately chose this longer timeframe to \"reduce the number of trades per year, thereby reducing transaction costs and the effects of slippage.\"\n\nThis suggests rebalancing occurs infrequently—only when the price crosses the moving-average threshold, which typically happens several times annually rather than on a fixed calendar schedule.\n\n## Summary\n\nThe overlay functions as a **regime filter** that completely switches between two assets (2x leveraged equities or short-duration treasuries) rather than dynamically scaling position size."}]},"uuid":"9932b6f9-dadf-4f9b-ad32-ac8c1078d06b","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:17.326Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"908c2dba-3513-4c5b-a74a-defd0ea1a8fa","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"c544e747-be3b-4b90-aacf-176833fbbb5a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011Ei5c7RGiXKH2FE2obXhYa","type":"tool_result","content":"# Dynamic Breakout II Strategy: Volatility-Adaptive Logic & Exit Rules\n\n## Lookback Window Adaptation\n\nThe strategy begins with a 20-day lookback period, then adjusts dynamically based on market volatility. As stated: \"We change the number of look back days in proportion to changes in market volatility.\"\n\nThe mechanism compares daily volatility shifts. The code calculates: \"the current market volatility is calculated by the standard deviation of the past 30 day's closing prices.\" The adjustment formula is: `self.numdays = round(self.numdays * (1 + deltavol))` where deltavol represents the proportional volatility change.\n\nCritically, \"the look back days are dynamic, it needs to be restricted within an acceptable range of 20 to 60.\"\n\n## Entry Rules\n\n**Buy Setup:** Requires two simultaneous conditions—\"the close price of the previous day must be above the upper Bollinger Band\" AND \"the ask price must be above the highest high of the most recent N days\" (where N = lookback period).\n\n**Sell Setup:** Mirror conditions—\"the close price of previous day must be below the lower Bollinger Band and the ask price must be below the lowest low of the most recent N days.\"\n\nBollinger Bands use k=2: Upper Band = μ + 2σ; Lower Band = μ − 2σ.\n\n## Exit Rules\n\nExits employ a **moving-average-based liquidation point**, not trailing stops. The strategy \"liquidate[s] a long position if the current price is lower than the moving average of the close price over the look back period, and vice versa for selling a short position.\"\n\nOperationally: `self.long_liq_point = np.mean(historyclose)` creates a dynamic exit threshold matching the adaptive lookback window."}]},"uuid":"c9133aba-f90f-4fed-aa85-bbf7f204a22c","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:22.337Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c544e747-be3b-4b90-aacf-176833fbbb5a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"ac583a9c-4d20-4b45-a65c-2597e010c110","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015WXowbm82aKRphikqoAjvX","type":"tool_result","content":"# G-Score Construction and Strategy Framework\n\n## Composite Score Building\n\nThe G-Score is constructed as a **sum of binary indicators**. Seven fundamental factors are evaluated; when each condition is met, one point is added, producing a score ranging from 0 to 7. The seven components are:\n\n1. Return on Assets (ROA) exceeding industry median\n2. Cash Flow Return on Assets (CFROA) exceeding industry median\n3. CFROA surpassing ROA\n4. ROA variance lower than industry median\n5. Research & Development spending above industry median\n6. Capital Expenditure above industry median\n7. Advertisement spending above industry median\n\n## Universe and Selection Criteria\n\nThe strategy starts with \"all companies that have fundamental data\" and applies multiple filters:\n- Focuses on Technology stocks exclusively\n- Narrows to companies in the bottom quartile by Book-to-Market ratio\n- **Only longs securities with G-Scores of 5 or higher**\n\n## Rebalance and Holding\n\nThe document does not explicitly specify rebalancing frequency or holding periods.\n\n## Data Requirements\n\n- MorningStar fundamental data (balance sheets, income statements, cash flow statements)\n- Book-to-Market calculations requiring net tangible assets and market capitalization\n- Twelve-month and three-month rolling financial metrics\n- Industry median benchmarks for comparative analysis"}]},"uuid":"9ea51d8e-776b-444b-bae3-03af288925a0","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:43.832Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ac583a9c-4d20-4b45-a65c-2597e010c110","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"0357799f-e77a-4a69-b3fe-89714be764fe","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FEsgqubPaXDkhn5cfbEp1s","type":"tool_result","content":"# Fama French Five-Factor Strategy: Computation and Implementation\n\n## Factor Computation\nThe strategy maps five fundamental metrics to the classic five factors:\n\n- **MKT (Market/Value)**: Book value per share\n- **SMB (Size)**: Total equity\n- **HML (Quality)**: Operation profit margin\n- **RMW (Profitability)**: Return on equity (ROE)\n- **CMA (Investment)**: Total assets growth\n\n## Combination Method\nRather than regression analysis, the approach uses a **weighted ranking system**:\n\n1. Securities are independently sorted by each factor\n2. Each stock receives a rank position (0 to N) within each factor's sorting\n3. Ranks are multiplied by corresponding beta weights (beta_m, beta_s, beta_h, beta_r, beta_c)\n4. The five weighted ranks are averaged into a single composite score\n5. Stocks are sorted by this composite score\n\n## Portfolio Construction\n- **Long positions**: Five stocks with highest composite scores\n- **Short positions**: Five stocks with lowest composite scores\n- This creates a market-neutral long/short portfolio\n\n## Universe and Requirements\n- **Initial filter**: Stocks with fundamental data and price > $5\n- **Coarse selection**: Top securities by dollar volume\n- **Fine selection**: Only stocks with all five required fundamentals available\n- **Rebalance frequency**: Every 30 days\n- **Data period tested**: January 2010 – August 2019\n\n## Performance\nThe backtest achieved approximately 6.8% annual returns with 19.8% maximum drawdown over the period."}]},"uuid":"f948affa-7399-42a5-86b0-6c4655b03954","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:47.352Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0357799f-e77a-4a69-b3fe-89714be764fe","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"d1d5b67d-4596-4c07-be41-46c4f9ffebfb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GdNNBn6P8HAc92yTQHa9xj","type":"tool_result","content":"# Optimal Pairs Trading: Entry/Exit Thresholds\n\n## Analytical Framework\n\nThe model uses an **Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) process** to characterize the spread between paired stocks. The approach derives optimal thresholds through **optimal stopping theory**, maximizing expected gains while accounting for transaction costs.\n\n## Threshold Derivation\n\n**Entry Threshold (d*):**\nThe optimal entry level is calculated by solving: \"G(d)(V'(d)-1)-G'(d)(V(d)-d-c)=0\" where G(x) and V(x) are specific integral functions defined in the model. This represents the portfolio value at which to initiate the position.\n\n**Exit Threshold (b*):**\nThe optimal liquidation level is derived by solving: \"F(b)-(b-c)F'(b)=0\" where F(x) is an integral function. This is computed first since it feeds into the entry calculation.\n\nBoth thresholds incorporate transaction costs (c = 0.05) and a discount rate (r = 0.05).\n\n## Exit Rule\n\n\"Once the hypothetical portfolio value we tracking reaches d*, we liquidate our positions.\" The exit is purely threshold-based—when the spread reaches the optimal liquidation level, the position closes.\n\n## Stop Mechanisms\n\nThe research **contains no stop-loss or time-stop provisions**. Exit occurs exclusively when the spread reaches the mathematically optimal liquidation threshold. The model retrains quarterly to update these thresholds based on recent price data."}]},"uuid":"410200fd-2a8b-4462-9179-8df596ad445d","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:49.317Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d1d5b67d-4596-4c07-be41-46c4f9ffebfb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"ffbd1a90-ab55-415b-8df3-5617a64a2a61","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019uc3EUcjNSbxC5beMi6td7","type":"tool_result","content":"# Earnings Quality Factor Score Construction\n\n## Components\nThe composite score integrates four earnings quality metrics:\n\n1. **Accruals** – calculated from balance sheet changes: \"(ΔCA - ΔCash) - (ΔCL - ΔSTD - ΔITP) - Dep\"\n2. **Cash Flow to Assets (CFA)** – operating cash flow divided by total earnings\n3. **Debt to Assets (DA)** – leverage indicator from operation ratios\n4. **Return on Equity (ROE)** – profitability measure\n\n## Rank Aggregation Method\nThe strategy employs ordinal rank aggregation. Each stock receives individual ranks across the four factors, then these ranks are summed into a composite score. The sorting directional logic prioritizes \"quality\" as: low accruals, high CFA, low leverage, and high ROE.\n\n## Universe & Rebalancing\nThe investment universe includes \"all non-financial stocks from NYSE, Amex and Nasdaq.\" Portfolio rebalancing occurs annually at \"the end of each June.\"\n\n## Position Allocation\nLong positions target \"the top 30% of high score stocks\" while short positions target \"the bottom 30% of low score stocks,\" with each side allocated 0.8 portfolio weight.\n\n## Data Requirements\nThe algorithm requires one year of historical fundamental data initialization before trading begins, using balance sheet, income statement, and cash flow statement items from consecutive fiscal years."}]},"uuid":"d11fb099-813d-4529-ba32-aa266b9b1d5f","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:46:51.838Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ffbd1a90-ab55-415b-8df3-5617a64a2a61","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

{"parentUuid":"63a463d1-f64e-4a53-a231-67230a3bade1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0167z5mFE5oSivHfkf5vrzrq","type":"tool_result","content":"# Apache License 2.0\n\n**License Name:** Apache License, Version 2.0 (January 2004)\n\n**Key Permissions:**\n- \"perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive, no-charge, royalty-free, irrevocable copyright license to reproduce, prepare Derivative Works of, publicly display, publicly perform, sublicense, and distribute\"\n- You may create derivative works and add your own attribution notices\n- You can distribute in Source or Object form with modifications\n\n**Key Restrictions:**\n- \"You must give any other recipients of the Work or Derivative Works a copy of this License\"\n- Modified files must carry prominent notices indicating changes\n- Must retain all copyright, patent, and attribution notices from the original work\n- \"This License does not grant permission to use the trade names, trademarks, service marks, or product names\"\n- No warranty provided: work is distributed \"AS IS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND\"\n- Patent licenses terminate if you initiate patent litigation against the licensor"}]},"uuid":"17657d79-d720-49c0-a76e-98009538a597","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:08.484Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"63a463d1-f64e-4a53-a231-67230a3bade1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"c071b790-6139-4e2b-992b-f026337eab91","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J5oAFyQXNpjbMBz2sdncTf","type":"tool_result","content":"# Asset Class Trend Following Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Moving Average Rule:**\nThe strategy employs a \"ten-month simple moving average\" to identify trends. When an asset's closing price exceeds its SMA, it signals a buy condition; when below, it signals a sell.\n\n**Position Gating (On/Off):**\nThis is a binary gate mechanism rather than scaling. Assets are either fully included or excluded—\"when the closing price is over its ten-month simple moving average, we give equal allocation to those ETFs, otherwise stay in cash.\"\n\n**Universe:**\nThe algorithm targets \"5 ETFs in different asset classes like stocks, bonds, and commodities.\"\n\n**Rebalance Frequency:**\nNot explicitly specified in the provided content.\n\n**Capital Allocation When Off:**\nWhen assets don't meet the moving average criterion, \"stay in cash\"—capital is held in cash rather than reallocated to other positions. For active positions, \"equal allocation\" is applied across qualifying ETFs.\n\n**Initialization:**\nA \"warm-up period of ten months\" primes the data before live trading begins, ensuring the indicator is properly initialized."}]},"uuid":"5d4449e6-59a8-49fb-8fd9-8a02fa0b9bc7","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:10.451Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c071b790-6139-4e2b-992b-f026337eab91","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"720c1843-84fd-45d9-9464-e0e9557ac37f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bdcc879e-db5e-4a63-9d8c-6ca5c95865e3","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PYLswFUkAbBS817Mj33bZz","type":"tool_result","content":"# VIX Percentile Trading Mechanism\n\n## Percentile Computation\nThe algorithm creates \"20 equally spaced percentiles for the history close price of VIX in the last two years,\" meaning it calculates the 5th, 10th, 15th... through 95th percentile levels using a rolling 2-year window of historical VIX closing prices.\n\n## Trading Thresholds\nTwo key decision points trigger position changes:\n- **Long signal**: When current VIX exceeds the 90th percentile\n- **Short signal**: When current VIX falls below the 10th percentile\n\n## Exposure Mechanism\nThe strategy gates exposure on/off rather than scaling it. Upon signal generation, it executes `set_holdings(\"OEF\", 1)` for long positions or `set_holdings(\"OEF\", -1)` for short positions—full allocation shifts with no partial sizing.\n\n## Holding Period\nThe research does not specify an explicit holding duration. Positions remain until the opposing signal triggers, suggesting a flexible holding period based on VIX movement relative to percentile thresholds.\n\n## Data Requirements\n- Two years of historical VIX daily closing prices (504 trading days)\n- Daily VIX data from the CBOE VIX Daily Price dataset\n- Daily price data for the S&P 100 ETF (OEF)\n- Backtesting period: January 2006 through August 2018"}]},"uuid":"cd798556-9190-4645-9088-65e3aae268de","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:12.314Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"720c1843-84fd-45d9-9464-e0e9557ac37f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}

The web page content provided appears to be empty or blank—there's no actual text between the content markers for me to analyze.\n\nTo help you understand how price momentum and earnings momentum strategies work, I would need:\n\n- An actual article, research paper, or strategy documentation\n- Details about the specific methodology being discussed\n- Information about the portfolio construction approach\n\nIf you could share the relevant content or provide a link to the material you're referencing, I'd be happy to explain the mechanism, including how these factors are combined, the investment universe, lookback periods, rebalancing frequency, holding periods, and data requirements—all while respecting the 125-character quote limit and proper attribution guidelines you've outlined."}]},"uuid":"f946b8c1-8181-474a-8a4b-d2fc8c22aa06","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:34.416Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9814cfe9-0b76-4c3e-a028-71fae8cdc246","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"4bac68a9-b789-4f57-be46-101d4f6dccfa","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JR9Xn2e7RXC6PEcksY6Pf6","type":"tool_result","content":"# QuantConnect Community Strategies Page\n\n## Overview\nThe Strategies page showcases algorithms contributed by community members. QuantConnect \"backtests these strategies on behalf of the community each day, allowing you to see it it performs with out-of-sample data.\"\n\n## Metrics and Scoring\nThe leaderboard ranks strategies using:\n- **Three-month return**: Performance over the past quarter\n- **One-year Sharpe ratio**: Risk-adjusted returns measure\n- **Score**: The primary ranking metric\n\nThe score calculation applies a penalty for newer strategies. Specifically, \"The score is the Sharpe ratio over one year with a penalty for strategies with less than one year of out-of-sample data.\" A strategy submitted over one year receives its full Sharpe ratio as the score, while newer strategies have their ratios reduced proportionally to their submission age.\n\n## Out-of-Sample Meaning\nIn this context, \"out-of-sample data\" refers to market performance that occurred after the strategy's submission. QuantConnect continuously tests submitted strategies against new, real-world market data to evaluate their genuine predictive value beyond their backtested period.\n\n## Number of Strategies\nThe documentation doesn't specify the total strategy count, only that the leaderboard displays \"the 10 highest ranked strategies.\"\n\n## Cloning and Sharing\nUsers can \"clone them into your own projects\" and contribute their own strategies. The documentation doesn't detail specific licensing terms for cloned strategies."}]},"uuid":"9bf3f6ea-2ad9-4ed5-8dbd-2d40b9304514","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:34.843Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4bac68a9-b789-4f57-be46-101d4f6dccfa","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"6b531b6a-a37c-4034-b6f4-4cfceb9cc5dc","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UGGLSTTyK9osoariVYEyfN","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum and Style Rotation Effect - Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Signal:** The strategy uses a single signal—the Momentum Percent (MOMP) indicator calculated over a 12-month formation period applied to six equity style ETFs.\n\n**Weights:** Not regime-conditional. The algorithm employs a fixed pair-trading structure: \"long the top performing ETF and short the ETF at the bottom\" with 0.5 weight allocations to each position.\n\n**Universe:** Six iShares ETFs representing equity styles:\n- Small-cap value (IJS), mid-cap value (IJJ), large-cap value (IVE)\n- Small-cap growth (IJT), mid-cap growth (IJK), large-cap growth (IVW)\n\n**Rebalance & Holding Period:** Monthly rebalancing occurs at the start of each month. Positions are \"held for one month,\" with the portfolio rebalanced upon the next month's arrival.\n\n**Data Requirements:** Daily price data for the six ETFs spanning the backtest period (2001-2018 in the example). The 12-month lookback window requires 240 trading days of historical data to calculate the momentum indicator.\n\n**Execution Logic:** \"Liquidate the ETFs that are no longer selected\" between rebalancing cycles, ensuring only the highest and lowest momentum performers remain in the portfolio."}]},"uuid":"b4eb226d-e0e2-4185-8a09-9e600ad73eb3","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:36.678Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6b531b6a-a37c-4034-b6f4-4cfceb9cc5dc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"7fd177aa-8e48-4d3b-9706-d226f16c0f4c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EukHsXPv5BNYPfDwqpcMW7","type":"tool_result","content":"# Momentum Effect in Stocks - Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Universe:** Large-cap stocks. The strategy employs \"coarse-universe selection\" eliminating stocks below $5 and ETFs lacking fundamental data, then applies \"fine-universe selection\" to choose \"the 50 largest companies ranked by market capitalization.\"\n\n**Momentum Lookback:** 12 months. The algorithm selects \"stocks with the best 12-month momentum (12-month performance).\"\n\n**Skip Month:** Not explicitly mentioned. The document describes monthly rebalancing without reference to skipping a month between momentum measurement and portfolio formation.\n\n**Rebalance Frequency:** Monthly. \"The portfolio is rebalanced once a month\" using universe selection logic that tracks the current month to trigger filtering on the first trading day of each month.\n\n**Holding Period:** One month (implied by monthly rebalancing).\n\n**Number of Stocks:** 50 stocks, as determined by the fine-universe selection process.\n\n**Weighting:** Equal weighting. Selections are \"weighted equally\" in the portfolio.\n\n**Data Requirements:** Daily closing prices for momentum calculation, market capitalization data for ranking, and Morningstar fundamental data for filtering."}]},"uuid":"870ae0a1-352e-4b49-827c-13bbee58f2c0","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:37.429Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7fd177aa-8e48-4d3b-9706-d226f16c0f4c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
