{"parentUuid":"148b4e81-b2c1-488a-a7db-c513232f4f81","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PDsjiD8pKtgB4hiRZ7DJGX","type":"tool_result","content":"# Price and Earnings Momentum Strategy Mechanism\n\n## Combination Method\nThe strategy employs a **score summation approach** rather than independent double sorting. Both indicators are ranked separately, then combined: \"Get the sum of rank for each symbol and pick the top ones to long and the bottom ones to short.\" The final ranking determines portfolio construction.\n\n## Universe Selection\n- **Coarse filter**: Top 100 most liquid equities with prices exceeding $5 and available fundamental data\n- **Fine filter**: Further refined based on combined momentum scores\n\n## Lookbacks and Periods\n- **Price momentum**: Calculated using quarterly returns (three-month historical close prices)\n- **Earnings momentum**: Based on quarter-over-quarter EPS growth using a rolling window of two periods\n\n## Rebalancing and Holding\n- **Frequency**: Quarterly rebalancing\n- **Holding period**: Positions maintained for approximately three months until next rebalancing cycle\n\n## Position Construction\n- Equal weighting across all selected securities\n- Long 10 highest-ranked stocks\n- Short 10 lowest-ranked stocks\n- Total portfolio: 20 positions with 1/(20) weight per position\n\n## Data Requirements\n- Daily closing prices for quarterly return calculations\n- Quarterly earnings per share (BasicEPS) data from fundamental datasets\n- Dollar volume data for coarse universe filtering\n\nThe backtest produced a Sharpe ratio of -0.268 versus 0.758 for the S&P 500 during the test period."}]},"uuid":"b8bccdd3-3712-4413-a0cb-6f70e8743cfc","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:58.070Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"148b4e81-b2c1-488a-a7db-c513232f4f81","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"4986d33a-e0cc-4168-92c7-3d298e24f426","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01W4EhPx7tP4Bi6HotLC5XY4","type":"tool_result","content":"# Asset Growth Effect Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Asset Growth Definition:**\nThe strategy measures annual changes in total assets, scaled by prior-year assets to account for firm size differences. As the research states, \"high asset growth stocks underperform stocks with low asset growth.\"\n\n**Universe Selection:**\nThe investment universe comprises non-financial U.S. equities on NYSE, AMEX, and NASDAQ with available fundamental data. The coarse selection filters for stocks with fundamental data availability, while fine selection requires positive total assets and excludes financial (B) and utility (I) sectors.\n\n**Rebalancing & Holding Period:**\nThe portfolio rebalances annually at the end of June, comparing current-year and prior-year balance sheet data.\n\n**Direction of Effect:**\nThis is a long-short strategy that takes long positions in the bottom decile of asset growth stocks (lowest growth) and short positions in the top decile (highest growth), betting that slower-growing companies outperform rapid-growth companies.\n\n**Data Requirements:**\nThe strategy requires two years of fundamental balance sheet data—specifically total assets figures from both the current and previous fiscal years—to calculate the annual growth metric used for ranking and portfolio construction."}]},"uuid":"0f8238fe-6312-4a39-85d3-2be7105fa166","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:58.450Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4986d33a-e0cc-4168-92c7-3d298e24f426","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"e9d5a34f-5e0c-434d-a0fc-e6dd7d7bcc28","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"bfeb96d1-a925-4b27-ab67-9fd7eeb5bd85","agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QCE4LaKeQevcDEbm2ndXha","type":"tool_result","content":"# Short-Term Reversal Strategy Mechanism\n\n**Reversal Lookback:** The strategy measures performance over the previous month using the \"Rate of Change indicator\" to calculate monthly returns.\n\n**Universe Selection:** Starting with stocks priced above $4, the strategy ranks by dollar volume and selects the \"top 100 stocks as our asset pool,\" then applies fundamentals-based filtering.\n\n**Fine Universe:** In the second screening stage, prescreened stocks are \"sorted by market cap we choose the top 20.\"\n\n**Rebalance Frequency & Holding Period:** The strategy rebalances monthly, with a one-month holding period aligned to the lookback window.\n\n**Weighting:** Equal-weighted positions—\"go long on the 10 stocks with the lowest performance in the previous month and go short on the 10 stocks with the greatest performance.\"\n\n**Data Requirements:** The approach relies on price data (for return calculations) and fundamental data (market capitalization). Historical pricing data enables the Rate of Change calculations driving the reversal signal.\n\n**Core Logic:** Exploiting the documented anomaly that recent losers tend to outperform and recent winners underperform in subsequent periods."}]},"uuid":"090d9f22-0a92-4b40-a1df-88ad1570a6f6","timestamp":"2026-08-02T22:47:59.988Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e9d5a34f-5e0c-434d-a0fc-e6dd7d7bcc28","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/erdemozturk/AI-Trading","sessionId":"70939c98-2d7b-4bea-aa38-9223f540792e","version":"2.1.219","gitBranch":"main","slug":"tidy-soaring-crown"}
{"parentUuid":"090d9f22-0a92-4b40-a1df-88ad1570a6f6","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a048b4740695e15a9","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdejfirwfsJPMY9mchYLw","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'm organizing the report structure with a honesty caveat upfront, then moving into the component-based lesson extraction section where I'll detail what QC winners actually do, provide relevant URLs, highlight what distinguishes them from the Meridian approach in one sentence, and include a measurable outcome statement for each of the six components. I need to be careful about how I label these as \"winners\" and stay truthful about the comparisons.\n\nLooking at QC's own reported numbers, most of their published backtests actually underperform SPY—commodities trend has a Sharpe of -0.131, price and earnings momentum sits at -0.268, mean-reversion strategies show 6% returns with 49% drawdown, and seasonality barely beats the benchmark at 0.128 versus SPY's 0.773. This is a critical finding: the catalog isn't a collection of winners but rather a mixed bag where the truly outperforming strategies are a small subset. Only a handful like Expected Idiosyncratic Skewness (0.947 Sharpe) and the Baltas-Kosowski improved momentum approach show genuine edge, so I'll frame this honestly in the report and note where I'm quoting QC's own claims versus what I've independently verified.\n\nNow I'm diving into the first component on exits and trailing stops, where I've found that across the entire cross-sectional equity library, exit mechanisms are driven by calendar logic rather than other triggers.\n\nThe strategies explicitly document having no stop-loss or time-based stops, but they do use indicator-crossing liquidation points like Dynamic Breakout II's moving mean exit level, analytically derived thresholds from optimal stopping theory in pairs trading, and regime-based asset rotation. The crucial distinction from Meridian's approach is that Meridian uses a ratcheting chandelier that only moves upward, whereas Dynamic Breakout II's exit level is a breathing mean that expands during high volatility instead of tightening—and this mechanism difference directly explains why Meridian gets whipsawed: a ratchet tightens precisely when volatility spikes, forcing exits at the worst possible moment.\n\nBeyond the exit mechanics, I'm also noticing partial exit strategies through overlapping calendar tranches—splitting a position into thirds or sixths across multiple months rather than entering and exiting all at once on a single signal. This is a portfolio-level partial exit structure that Meridian doesn't employ.\n\nFor sizing and risk management, the most sophisticated approach I'm seeing is the Baltas-Kosowski TSMOM-CF framework, which combines three multiplicative factors: trend strength derived from the t-statistic of 12-month daily returns, inverse volatility scaling using the Yang-Zhang estimator against a 12% portfolio target, and a correlation adjustment factor that accounts for diversification benefits across the portfolio.\n\nThe key distinction from Meridian's EDG-008 work is that Baltas-Kosowski applies inverse-vol scaling cross-sectionally within each position for risk parity rather than as a time-series overlay on the entire book, and introduces a separate correlation factor that reduces gross exposure when positions move together—this is fundamentally a diversification mechanism, not a volatility measure, so there's no actual conflict with EDG-008's findings about time-series vol-scaling directionality.\n\nI'm also noting that Yang-Zhang volatility estimation incorporates overnight gaps and intraday range, whereas Meridian likely uses close-to-close returns, and since Yang-Zhang is computable from daily OHLC data it's accessible without proprietary inputs. The rebalancing approach decouples holding period from rebalance frequency through overlapping tranches, following the Jegadeesh-Titman framework that QC implements across multiple tranches. Meridian's testing showed no difference in rebalance frequency itself, but that's separate from the tranche structure question. The key insight is that using overlapping tranches instead of a single monthly rebalance reduces base-case variance while keeping the same mean — the improvement shows up in confidence interval width, not average returns. Excluding the final five trading days of each month to avoid month-end microstructure effects doesn't meaningfully change or degrade the base case.\n\nLooking at how other QC equity strategies actually construct their universes, they all follow a similar two-stage dynamic funnel: first a coarse screen for liquid, fundamentally-sound stocks above a price floor, then ranking by dollar volume to select a small top-N set (typically 20 to 100, occasionally 200), followed by a fine filter on market cap and sector exclusions that runs monthly. Meridian's static 251-stock universe is quite different, and the fact that professional strategies use dollar volume as the primary liquidity screen before market cap suggests Meridian's turnover edge might be coming from that liquidity-first approach.\n\nThe key insight is that Meridian discovered turnover as a ranking signal, but if the universe itself is already constructed to be dollar-volume-top-N, some of that edge could already be baked in. A simpler test would be switching to a dynamic dollar-volume-based universe instead of the fixed 251 to see if that captures the same benefit more efficiently. Also worth noting: most fundamental strategies exclude financials and utilities by sector, which Meridian doesn't do.\n\nNow looking at how signals combine into scores—I'm seeing three main approaches: a binary flag system that sums conditions (like Piotroski's method, simple and K-friendly), rank-sum approaches where factors are ranked and summed together, and presumably other weighting schemes.\n\nThen there's a fundamentally different structure: double-sorting, where one signal creates the initial grouping (momentum deciles or volatility quintiles) and a second signal ranks within those subgroups. This is conditional rather than additive—the second signal only matters within the context established by the first, which contrasts with Meridian's approach of combining scores additively.\n\nMeridian already tested the momentum-turnover interaction directly and found the main effect of turnover dominates, so that particular pairing is settled. But the volatility-first conditioning hasn't been explored yet. I also notice that across all these methods, none actually use regime-conditional weighting—I checked thoroughly and found nothing beyond a straightforward momentum pair trade labeled as style rotation. That's a genuine gap in the catalog.\n\nNow looking at regime constructs more broadly: every single one functions as a binary gate rather than a continuous size modulator. Whether it's the Wilshire momentum signal switching between full momentum and 100% bonds, the asset class trend filter toggling positions in or out of cash, or the leveraged ETF approach flipping between leveraged long and short—they're all on-off switches, not graduated adjustments.\n\nThere's one genuine exception: the Baltas-Kosowski trend rule uses the t-statistic of 12-month returns clipped to the [-1,1] range as an actual continuous exposure modulator, scaling from proportional exposure up to 100% when the signal strength exceeds 1. This is the only catalog instance where trend strength directly modulates position size rather than just triggering entry or exit. A weaker second exception exists in the momentum and leveraged ETF regimes, which rotate to bonds rather than cash when off, so the \"off\" state still generates returns—though Meridian's gate framework presumably accounts for this distinction.\n\nI should also flag that the Wilshire 5000 FRED series is discontinued, which means the original article has a broken data dependency; SPY serves as a reasonable free proxy for testing purposes.\n\nThe Strategy Library contains about 85 GitHub repositories with 83 published tutorial articles, though the numbering is scattered across Quantpedia IDs and QC's own native IDs. Each entry includes a written thesis with academic citations, complete Python source code (and historically C# as well), plus a backtest using QC's metrics, all available under Apache 2.0 on GitHub. There's also a separate Strategy Explorer that surfaces community algorithms ranked by one-year Sharpe ratio with penalties for insufficient out-of-sample data, which QC rebalances daily through live backtesting.\n\nOn licensing: the tutorials repository permits derivative works with attribution and modification notices under Apache 2.0, while the website content itself is copyrighted by QuantConnect, and community strategies lack explicit licensing so I'm treating them as read-only reference material rather than copy-able source. Now I'm tallying the actual count of library entries and mapping overlaps with existing documentation, starting with the liquidity effect strategies. ived) — assets versus shares variant\n8. Accrual Anomaly sits near the gross profitability family (EDG-014, archived) in the quality space\n9. ROA Effect and Earnings Quality Factor both belong to EDG-014 (archived), with the latter using a rank-sum composite approach\n10. Volatility effects span EDG-008 and the MAX strategy (EDG-004, archived), including a variant that conditions on volatility quintiles\n11. Momentum Effect in Stocks is still active\n\n, while smaller-cap momentum (N=10, matching Meridian's setup) and time series momentum connect to EDG-009 and EDG-008\n12. Calendar anomalies like the Pre-Holiday and January Effects are archived, sitting near the kill-list\n13. Momentum-reversal dynamics span from short-term reversals (adjacent to the archived EDG-010 pullback) through style rotation within the momentum family\n14. Value and fundamental factors—book-to-market, price-earnings, FF5 components, and sentiment—cluster together, some locked to point-in-time data or gross profitability considerations\n15. Small-cap premium sits in EDG-018 mid-cap territory but is currently suspended due to data constraints\n16. Beta and volatility factors form their own family\n17. Now I'm looking at seasonality patterns—same-calendar-month and 12-month cycles—which appear to be new candidates for bucket B\n\nI need to systematically classify all 83 strategies by bucket rather than estimate. Starting with CAPM alpha on the Dow 30, which uses monthly alpha regression on the top 2 performers—this mechanism overlaps with EDG-007's residual momentum since CAPM alpha over short horizons is essentially residual return momentum, though the exact relationship needs clarification.\n\nThe key distinction is that EDG-007 measured 12-month residual momentum while this approach uses a 21-day horizon, which tends toward reversal rather than momentum. I'll classify it as a variant in bucket A with a note about the horizon difference. Moving through the remaining strategies: forex mean reversion and momentum combinations go to bucket C, pairs trading with copula versus cointegration methods also go to bucket C as market-neutral long-short strategies, and dynamic breakout approaches belong in bucket C as intraday/futures strategies.\n\nFor crude oil predicting equity returns, the issue is that it's fundamentally index-level timing rather than cross-sectional stock selection—a regime classification problem. Even though the data is freely available, the application is regime-based, so I'm putting it in bucket C with that reasoning noted.\n\nMoving through the remaining strategies: intraday pairs trading and low-frequency forex momentum both fall into C, while the fundamental factor strategies—stock selection based on fundamentals, short-term reversals, and fundamental long-short—all belong in the A family.\n\nAsset class trend following and momentum strategies are C-category ETF approaches, but sector momentum is interesting because I can extract a B-category idea from it: sector-relative momentum, which measures a stock's momentum against its sector's momentum. This is computable from freely available data if I can access sector classifications, though that introduces some data dependency risk I'll need to flag.\n\nShort-term reversal is straightforward A-category. Overnight anomalies are worth exploring as a B-category feature—decomposing overnight versus intraday returns from daily OHLC data is something Meridian hasn't measured before. Forex momentum and carry are C-category strategies, while volatility effects in stocks fall into the A-category volatility family, and pairs trading is a C-category long-short approach.\n\nContinuing through the list: momentum effects in stocks tie to the EDG-009 family (A), momentum and mean reversion in country indexes are both C-category, liquidity effects in stocks are A-category with EDG-016 as a living mechanism variant, and volatility risk premium through options is C-category. Commodities futures momentum is C-category, small cap premium is A-category adjacent to EDG-018, and term structure strategies for commodities are C-category, whether standalone or combined with momentum. Book-to-market value factors are A-category with PIT considerations.\n\nMoving forward, I'm looking at gold market timing as C-category, paired switching strategies using ETF rotation as C-category, momentum combined with short-term reversal as A-category, turn of the month equity indexes tied to EDG-006 as A-category, and sentiment-driven style rotation as C-category with data lock constraints on the sentiment index. Momentum filtered by market state is A-category as an EDG-005 variant, accrual anomaly is A-category within the quality family with PIT, asset growth effect is A-category neighboring EDG-012, and pairs trading across country ETFs is C-category.\n\nNow examining VIX-based index timing as C-category with component-6 notes and accessible VIX data, lunar cycles in emerging markets as C-category with no clear mechanism, combining momentum with volume as A-category related to EDG-013/016 variants, short-term reversal using futures as C-category, and beta factors in stocks as A-category in the volatility/beta family. I'm noticing beta hasn't been measured by Meridian or included in the card list, and low-beta anomaly appears to overlap with the volatility effect from strategy 18, which sits adjacent to EDG-004 MAX with reversed direction and EDG-008.\n\nMeridian's MAX finding showed high MAX performing better, which aligns with the \"high beta better\" direction but contradicts the low-beta anomaly—that's a genuinely interesting consistency check, so I'm marking 77 as B-category since beta is distinct from MAX and idiosyncratic volatility. Pre-holiday effect falls into A-category as part of the calendar family, momentum in mutual fund returns is C-category using mutual fund data, momentum and style rotation is C-category as an ETF pair trade that turned out to be ETF-only, and price-earnings anomaly is A-category in the value/PIT family.\n\nNow working through option expiration week effect—it's technically a calendar anomaly on equities tied to the third Friday, so it could be B-category, but Meridian's calendar family approach didn't work out and opex week is a distinct microstructure calendar effect with limited data availability, so marking it as B with low priority and prior skepticism. January barometer is C-category for index timing, January effect in stocks is A-category as a calendar family effect, time series momentum is A-category under EDG-009/008, the 12-month cycle in cross-section is a new B-category finding, residual momentum is A-category under EDG-007, and momentum in REITs is C-category as a sector-specific effect.\n\nContinuing through the list—momentum combined with reversal and volatility is an A-category variant using conditional sorting, momentum in small portfolios is an A-category variant of EDG-009, VIX futures term structure is C-category for futures, ROA effect is A-category for quality/point-in-time, value effect within countries is C-category, and mean-reversion stat arb using PCA is potentially B-category—it's a residual mean-reversion strategy on the top-20 dollar volume stocks at short horizons, adaptable to long-only by taking only the most negative z-scores, using just price data with no cost, though Meridian measured residual momentum over 12 months rather than the short-term reversion this captures.\n\nMoving forward, earnings quality factor is A-category quality/point-in-time, seasonality within the same calendar month is B-category and new, risk premia in forex is C-category, price and earnings momentum is A-category in the PEAD family adjacent to the kill-list, Fama-French five factors is A-category as a fundamental composite, expected idiosyncratic skewness is B-category and new—distinct because it forecasts skewness from lagged implied volatility and idiosyncratic skewness rather than using realized maximum returns, SUE is A-category on the PEAD kill-list, and improved momentum for commodities is starting to appear.\n\nContinuing through the list, commodities trend following is C-category, intraday arbitrage on index ETFs is C-category and intraday-focused, temporal CNN is C-category with machine learning complexity and K explosion concerns, leveraged ETF risk management is C-category with an ETF component note, intraday ETF momentum is C-category and intraday, news sentiment for drug manufacturers is C-category with NLP locked, Ichimoku clouds applied to the energy sector is C-category and sector-restricted technical analysis, and optimal pairs trading is coming up next. Continuing the count for category (a), I'm getting 29 total items. Now I'm identifying new candidates for category (b), including sector-relative momentum adaptations, overnight anomalies, beta factors, and various cyclical and seasonal patterns. Continuing to verify the count: I'm working through the remaining entries in category (c) and confirming the arithmetic. The total checks out at 82 strategies plus 1 index page for 83 articles overall. Now I'm shifting focus to ranking the top priorities in category (b) based on mechanism novelty compared to existing approaches, data availability, suitability for large-cap daily trading with a 10-20 day horizon, and implementation risk.\n\nThe first candidate is a PCA-residual mean reversion strategy using only price data from the top 20 dollar volume stocks—a genuinely short-horizon approach. While Meridian's archive shows residual momentum work over 12 months, short-horizon residual reversion at this scale is novel territory. The adaptation to long-only is straightforward, though the kill risk is moderate to high since short-term reversals in large caps face heavy arbitrage competition and Meridian's similar pullback strategy failed. Still, it's the most cost-effective genuinely new mechanism to explore.\n\nI'm reconsidering whether to pursue this given the adjacent short-term reversal family and Meridian's track record with pullback strategies, but I'm moving on to evaluate the second candidate around expected idiosyncratic skewness.\n\nThe second option uses realized maximum daily returns versus a forecasted skewness metric derived from lagged implied volatility and lagged idiosyncratic skewness through cross-sectional regression. What makes this compelling is that Meridian's MAX finding showed the opposite directional relationship—high realized max returns were actually better at the 20-day horizon—whereas this skewness forecast predicts the opposite pattern. This creates a direct, high-information test: was Meridian's MAX reversal driven by lottery preference or an implied volatility artifact? The data requirements are minimal—just prices and Fama-French factors from the public library. This approach has real explanatory power because it can directly account for an existing anomaly in the archive.\n\nThe third candidate is a simple seasonality pair using same-calendar-month returns as a cross-sectional signal, requiring only price data.\n\nFor the fourth option, I'm decomposing daily returns into overnight (close-to-open) and intraday (open-to-close) components using OHLC data. Meridian hasn't explored this decomposition before, and the literature on common factors in return seasonalities suggests this could be a genuinely different axis than their existing time-series calendar work. The monthly horizon fits reasonably well with the 20-day contract window, and the kill risk is moderate.\n\nFor the fifth option, I'm weighing the low-beta anomaly against sector-relative momentum. The beta approach has poor priors since the volatility family is heavily archived with mixed results, whereas sector-relative momentum is a natural refinement of Meridian's existing momentum edge—just demeaning returns by sector. The main concern is ensuring proper point-in-time sector classification, but this feels like a higher-conviction direction.\n\nReconsidering the full ranking, the highest-value candidates are those that either explain existing archive results or test genuinely new axes with minimal cost. The overnight decomposition stands out as particularly cheap and novel—it's a feature rather than a full strategy, so it fits naturally into the broader framework.\n\nNow I'm looking at the actual backtest numbers from QC's own reporting to make the point that this catalog isn't a curated winners list. The performance metrics show a wide range, from negative Sharpes on trend following and momentum strategies to modest positive returns on seasonality, all underperforming the benchmark.\n\nThe critical insight here is that across the six write-ups where QC published direct comparisons, five of them underperform SPY by their own numbers—354 EIS is the lone exception with a Sharpe of 0.947 versus SPY's 0.87. This means the tutorial library isn't showcasing mature, battle-tested strategies but rather demonstrating engineering conventions and implementation patterns that the literature community uses.\n\nThe Strategy Explorer is where the actual winners live, scored on one-year Sharpe with out-of-sample penalties, but I couldn't enumerate it because it's JavaScript-rendered and account-gated. Rapor:\n\n---\n\n# QuantConnect Strategy Library → Meridian eşlemesi\n\n## 0. DÜRÜSTLÜK ŞERHİ (önce okunmalı — çerçeveyi değiştiriyor)\n\nQC yazımlarının performans iddiaları bizim yasalarımızdan geçmedi; dersler **mekanizma düzeyinde** alındı, sayı taşınmadı. Ama tarama sırasında bundan daha sert bir şey çıktı:\n\n**Kataloğun \"kazanan\" kümesi neredeyse boş.** QC'nin kendi yazımında benchmark kıyası bastığı 6 stratejinin 5'i, QC'nin KENDİ sayılarıyla SPY'nin altında:\n\n| Strateji | QC'nin kendi bastığı sayı |\n|---|---|\n| 357 Commodities Trend Following | Sharpe **−0,131** |\n| 271 Price & Earnings Momentum | Sharpe **−0,268** (SPY 0,758) |\n| 269 Seasonality same-month | Sharpe **0,128** (SPY 0,773) |\n| 211 Mean-Reversion StatArb | ~%6/yıl, maxDD **~%49** |\n| 353 Fama-French 5 Factor | ~%6,8/yıl, maxDD %19,8 |\n| 354 Expected Idio. Skewness | Sharpe 0,947 (SPY 0,87) — **tek net geçen** |\n\nSonuç: Strategy Library bir **kazanan deposu değil, literatür-uygulama deposu**. Dolayısıyla aşağıdaki bileşen dersleri \"başarısı kanıtlanmış desen\" olarak değil, **mühendislik konvansiyonu** olarak etiketlenmeli — \"bu alanı uygulayanlar şunu yapıyor\", \"bu para kazanıyor\" değil. Gerçek \"kazanan\" listesi **Strategy Explorer**'da (community canlı, skor = 1-yıl Sharpe + <1yıl OOS cezası, QC her gün yeniden backtest ediyor) ve orayı **enumerate EDEMEDİM** — JS-render + hesap kapısı. **Operatör QC hesabını 2026-08-03'te açtı (EDG-021 notu): Explorer envanteri artık erişilebilir ve asıl kazanan-madenciliği ORADA.** Bu, bu turun kapanmamış tek kapısı.\n\n---\n\n## A. BİLEŞEN-BAZLI DERS ÇIKARIMI (asıl teslimat)\n\n### (1) ÇIKIŞ / TRAILING MİMARİSİ\n\n**Katalog bulgusu — güçlü ve tek yönlü:** Kesitsel hisse kütüphanesinin TAMAMINDA çıkış **takvim-kaynaklı**, durak-kaynaklı değil. Stop-loss'un açıkça yok olduğu yazımlar: [Commodities Futures Trend Following](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/commodities-futures-trend-following) (\"no stops, no trailing stops, no time-based exits\"), [Optimal Pairs Trading](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/optimal-pairs-trading) (\"no stop-loss or time-stop provisions\"), [Improved Momentum on Commodities Futures](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/improved-momentum-strategy-on-commodities-futures) (yalnız ±%100 clip). 83 yazımda ratchet'li trailing stop **bulunamadı**.\n\n**En bilgilendirici istisna — [Dynamic Breakout II](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/the-dynamic-breakout-ii-strategy):** çıkış eşiği = uyarlanan lookback penceresinin kapanış ORTALAMASI, ve pencere volatiliteyle birlikte 20–60 gün arasında **genişliyor** (`numdays = round(numdays * (1 + deltavol))`).\n\n- **Bizim tasarımla FARK (tek cümle):** Bizim chandelier bir RATCHET — tek yönlü yukarı kilitleniyor; DBII'nin eşiği ratchet DEĞİL ve vol arttığında **gevşiyor**, yani tam da bizim \"durak maliyeti\" bulgumuzun (chandelier kapatınca CAGR artıyor) mekanizma açıklaması: ratchet, vol genişlemesinde tam ters yönde sıkışıp en kötü anda çıkartıyor.\n- **Kart cümlesi:** \"Ratchet olmayan, volatiliteyle GENİŞLEYEN çıkış eşiği (uyarlanan N-gün ortalaması, N∈[20,60], vol-oranıyla ölçekli), chandelier ratchet'ine kıyasla ~63g tutuşta taban-fazlasını kaybetmeden maxDD'yi kötüleştirmez — hüküm: taban-fazlası KORUNUP maxDD düşerse P3 çıkış-paketi hükmü mekanizma-düzeyinde revize edilir.\"\n\n**İkinci ders — KISMİ ÇIKIŞ olarak örtüşük takvim dilimleri:** [Combining Momentum with Volume](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/combining-momentum-effect-with-volume) 3-ay tutuşu **her ay 1/3 dilim** ekleyip 3-ay-eskiyi tasfiye ederek kuruyor; [Momentum+Reversal+Volatility](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/momentum-and-reversal-combined-with-volatility-effect-in-stocks) 6-ay tutuşu **1/6 aylık dilimle**. Bu, portföy düzeyinde kısmi-çıkış.\n\n- **FARK:** Bizde giriş/çıkış pozisyon-atomik (tek seferde tam 1R girip tam çıkıyoruz); dilim yapısı tutuş süresini rebalans frekansından **ayırıyor**.\n- **Kart cümlesi:** \"Aynı sinyalle ~63g tutuşu tek-seferde değil 1/3'lük aylık örtüşük dilimlerle kurmak, taban-fazlası ORTALAMASINI değiştirmeden CI'yı daraltır — hüküm ortalamada değil CI genişliğinde ve giriş-zamanlaması varyansında aranır.\"\n\n### (2) BOYUTLAMA / RİSK\n\n**Katalogdaki en olgun boyutlama makinesi:** [Baltas-Kosowski TSMOM-CF](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/improved-momentum-strategy-on-commodities-futures) — ağırlık ÜÇ ÇARPANIN çarpımı:\n\n1. **Sürekli trend-gücü** `X ∈ [−1,+1]`: 12-aylık günlük log-getirilerin **t-istatistiği**; |t|>1 → %100 maruziyet, |t|<1 → orantılı kısılma.\n2. **Ters-vol**: `σ_hedef / σ_i`, portföy hedef vol %12, birim vol **Yang-Zhang** tahmincisiyle (gecelik boşluk + gün-içi aralık dahil).\n3. **Korelasyon çarpanı**: `CF(ρ̄) = √(N / (1 + (N−1)ρ̄))`, ρ̄ = son 3 ayın ortalama ikili korelasyonu.\n\n**Bizim EDG-008 hükmüyle ÇELİŞİYOR MU? — Hayır, ve bu ayrım önemli.** EDG-008 vol-scaling'i **zaman-serisi** kaldıracı olarak ölçtü (Moreira-Muir / Barroso-Santa-Clara: defterin tamamını kendi geçmiş volüne göre ölçekle) ve yönsüz buldu. Baltas-Kosowski'nin (2) maddesi **kesitsel risk-paritesi** (sepet İÇİNDE isimler arası), (3) maddesi ise vol değil **çeşitlenme** ölçüsü. Yani EDG-008 hükmü ayakta kalır; ama **iki ayrı ölçülmemiş kol** var.\n\n- **FARK:** Bizde sabit 1R + zaman-serisi de-risk rampası (günlerin %92'sinde aktif, işlem-boğucu ölçüldü); burada isim-bazlı ters-vol + korelasyon-bazlı brüt kısıntı, ve rampa yerine **rejim değil çeşitlenme** kaldıracı çekiliyor.\n- **Kart cümlesi A:** \"Aday sepeti içinde eşit-1R yerine ters-vol ağırlık (Yang-Zhang, günlük OHLC'den — ek veri gerekmez), aynı 5R ısı tavanı altında @20/@63 taban-fazlasını artırır.\"\n- **Kart cümlesi B (daha yüksek değerli):** \"Ortalama ikili korelasyon ρ̄(63g) yükseldiğinde brüt maruziyeti CF(ρ̄)=√(N/(1+(N−1)ρ̄)) ile kısmak, günlerin %92'sinde aktif olan işlem-boğucu de-risk rampasının yerini alarak maxDD'yi işlem sayısını boğmadan düşürür — hüküm: işlem sayısı KORUNURKEN maxDD düşerse rampa emekli edilir.\"\n\n### (3) TUTUŞ / REBALANS\n\n**Desen:** Ufuk sinyalin veri-tazelenme hızına kilitli, keyfi değil — fiyat-sinyalleri aylık ([Momentum Effect in Stocks](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/momentum-effect-in-stocks), [Short Term Reversal](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/short-term-reversal)), kazanç-sinyalleri çeyreklik, bilanço-sinyalleri **yıllık ve Haziran sonunda** ([Earnings Quality](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/earnings-quality-factor), [Asset Growth](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/asset-growth-effect) — Fama-French konvansiyonu, PIT güvenliği için).\n\n**Hijyen detayı:** 155 sinyal penceresinden **ay-sonu öncesi son haftayı dışlıyor** (\"to avoid biases due to microstructures\").\n\n- **FARK:** Bizim aylık-rebalans rafinesi \"farksız\" çıktı — ama ölçülen REBALANS FREKANSIYDI, dilim YAPISI değil; ayrıca sinyal penceresinin kuyruğunu kesme hijyenimiz yok.\n- **Kart cümlesi:** \"Sinyal penceresinin son 5 gününü dışlamak (ay-sonu mikroyapı/likidite artefaktı) taban-fazlasını düşürmez; düşmüyorsa sinyal mikroyapıya değil bilgiye dayanıyor demektir — bu bir POZİTİF KONTROL kartı, edge kartı değil.\"\n\n### (4) EVREN KURULUMU\n\n**Desen — istisnasız:** iki aşamalı **dinamik** huni, her ay yeniden koşuyor. Coarse: fiyat > $4–5, fundamental var, ADR/ETF/kapalı-uçlu fon hariç → **dolar-hacim üst-N** (modal N = 100; 20/200 de var). Fine: piyasa değeri üst-K, sektör dışlaması (finans + kamu hizmetleri, kalite/muhasebe kartlarında standart). Nihai portföy 10–50 isim.\n\n**En keskin gözlem:** QC evreni **dolar-hacimle KURUYOR**; bizim YAŞAYAN kenarımız (EDG-016 turnover üst-%20) aynı ailedeki değişkeni **SIRALAYICI** olarak kullanıyor. Bu, \"seçici mi sıralayıcı mı\" sorusunu doğuruyor — statik 251'de turnover sıralayıcı olarak para getiriyor; dinamik evrende aynı bilginin bir kısmı evren düzeyinde zaten tüketilmiş olabilir.\n\n- **FARK:** Bizim 251 statik-yakın; QC'nin evreni her ay yeniden doğuyor ve likidite ekseni evrenin İÇİNE gömülü.\n- **Kart cümlesi:** \"Statik 251 yerine aylık dolar-hacim üst-N dinamik evren kurulduğunda EDG-016 turnover üst-dilim kenarı KÜÇÜLÜR (bilgi evrene taşınmıştır) ya da KORUNUR (bilgi sıralamada) — iki tasarım yan yana ölçülür; fark, kenarın nereden geldiğinin doğrudan ölçüsüdür.\" *(Not: bu kart EDG-021 QC delist doğrulamasıyla aynı motorda koşabilir — evren tanımı zaten orada tartışılıyor.)*\n- **İkinci kart cümlesi:** \"Finans + kamu-hizmeti sektörlerinin evrenden dışlanması (muhasebe/kalite ailesinde evrensel konvansiyon) taban-fazlasını değiştirmez.\"\n\n### (5) SİNYAL BİRLEŞİMİ / SKOR\n\nKatalogda **dört ayrı** birleşim deseni, karmaşıklık sırasıyla:\n\n- **(a) İkili-bayrak toplamı** — [G-Score](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/g-score-investing): 7 koşul, her biri 1 puan, 0–7 tamsayı, yalnız ≥5 alınır. Ağırlık yok, uydurulacak parametre yok.\n- **(b) Sıra-toplamı (rank-sum)** — [Earnings Quality](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/earnings-quality-factor) (4 faktörün ordinal sıraları toplanır), [Price & Earnings Momentum](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/price-and-earnings-momentum) (2 sıra toplanır).\n- **(c) Ağırlıklı sıra-ortalaması** — [Fama-French 5](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/fama-french-five-factors): sıra × beta_i, ortalanır. **Tek uydurulan-ağırlıklı desen ve QC'nin kendi sayısıyla en zayıflarından biri (%6,8/yıl, %19,8 maxDD).**\n- **(d) KOŞULLU ARDIŞIK SIRALAMA (double sort)** — 66: önce momentum decile, SONRA o decile İÇİNDE turnover; 155: önce vol quintile, SONRA üst-vol quintile İÇİNDE 6-ay getiri. Toplamsal değil, **koşullandırıcı** yapı.\n\n- **FARK:** Bizim skor toplamsal-ekonomik (PARA-v3) + bileşen-IC. (d) deseni \"ikinci sinyal yalnız birincinin tanımladığı alt-popülasyonda bilgilidir\" der. **Bu bizde kısmen CEVAPLI:** EDG-013 (mom×turnover etkileşimi) arşivlendi, EDG-016 ana-etkiyi kurdu — yani mom×turnover çifti için toplamsal ana-etki koşullandırmayı yendi. Ama **vol-quintile-önce** koşullandırması (155) hiç ölçülmedi.\n- **Kart cümlesi A:** \"İkili-bayrak toplamı tarzı monoton tamsayı skor (0–K, eşik ≥m), sürekli-ağırlıklı skora kıyasla K-cezası ucuz ve aşırı-uydurmaya dayanıklı; mevcut PARA-v3 sıralamasıyla yan yana @20 taban-fazlası kıyaslanır.\"\n- **Kart cümlesi B:** \"Üst-vol quintile İÇİNDE momentum sıralaması, tüm-evren momentum sıralamasından fazla taban-fazlası üretir (155 koşullandırması) — EDG-004'ün ters-yön MAX bulgusuyla aynı yönde bir öngörü taşıdığı için ucuz bir tutarlılık sınavıdır.\"\n- **NEGATİF BULGU (dürüstlük):** Katalogda **rejim-koşullu sinyal AĞIRLIĞI örneği YOK.** 91 \"Style Rotation\" adına rağmen düz bir momentum çift-işlemi çıktı. Yani (5)+(6) kesişimi için katalogdan alınacak ders yok.\n\n### (6) REJİM\n\n**Katalogdaki rejim kurgularının hepsi KAPI:** [Momentum & State of Market](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/momentum-and-state-of-market-filters) (Wilshire 12-ay getiri işareti → momentum AÇIK, değilse %100 TLT), [Asset Class Trend Following](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/asset-class-trend-following) (10-ay SMA → içerde ya da nakit), [Leveraged ETFs](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/leveraged-etfs-with-systematic-risk-management) (200-gün SMA → SSO ya da SHY; yazım açıkça \"complete on/off switch, not a position-size modulator\" diyor), [VIX Predicts](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/vix-predicts-stock-index-returns) (90./10. persentil → tam long/tam short).\n\n**TEK istisna — ve tam da aradığınız desen:** Baltas-Kosowski **TREND kuralı**. 12-aylık günlük log-getirilerin t-istatistiği [−1,+1] aralığına kırpılarak **sürekli maruziyet çarpanı** olarak kullanılıyor. Kapı değil, **kadran**. Bu, bizim EDG-005 hükmümüzle (\"SMA kapısı volü düşürür ama parayı da düşürür\") **uyumlu** olan katalogdaki tek yapı.\n\n**İkinci, daha zayıf ders:** 37 ve 1025'te \"kapalı\" durum nakit değil **tahvil** — kapatılan risk boşta durmuyor.\n\n- **FARK:** Bizim SMA-200 kapımız ikili ve kapalı durumda düz; burada rejim sürekli bir ÇARPAN ve kapalı durum getiri üretiyor.\n- **Kart cümlesi (bu turun en yüksek değerli rejim kartı):** \"SPY 12-ay günlük log-getirisinin t-istatistiği, [−1,+1] kırpılmış SÜREKLİ maruziyet çarpanı olarak (kapı DEĞİL) uygulandığında, taban-fazlası KORUNURKEN maxDD düşer → EDG-005 hükmü 'kapı yanlış, modülasyon doğru' diye daraltılır; taban-fazlası da düşerse EDG-005 hükmü ('rejim müdahalesi parayı düşürür') mekanizmadan bağımsız olarak TEYİT edilir ve rejim ailesi kapanır.\"\n- **Veri şerhi:** QC'nin kullandığı Wilshire 5000 FRED serisi **kapatılmış** — yazımın kendi veri bağımlılığı ölü. Ücretsiz vekil: SPY. Bu, QC yazımlarının bakımsız olabileceğinin de kanıtı.\n\n---\n\n## B. KATALOG YAPISI\n\n- **Envanter:** GitHub'da 85 klasör; bunların 2'si dizin sayfası (`00 Strategy Library`, `26 Quantpedia`) → **83 yayımlı yazım, 82 gerçek strateji**. Numaralandırma bitişik değil: Quantpedia kaynaklı ID'ler (58, 61, 66 … 357) + QC-özgün seri (1023–1036).\n- **Her girdinin yapısı:** düzyazı makale (tez + akademik atıf) + tam Python kaynak (eski girdilerde C# de) + gömülü QC backtest'i ve kendi metrikleri. Kategori/etiket taksonomisi **yok** — düz liste.\n- **Lisans (net):** `QuantConnect/Tutorials` deposu **Apache License 2.0** — türev eser serbest; şartlar: lisans kopyası, değişiklik bildirimi, telif/atıf notlarının korunması; ticari marka izni verilmiyor; garanti yok. Site içeriği ayrıca \"© QuantConnect, All Rights Reserved\". **Community Strategy Explorer stratejileri için açık lisans belgelenmemiş** → oradan kopya alınmaz, yalnız fikir okunur.\n- **İki AYRI şey karıştırılmamalı:** `/tutorials/strategy-library/*` = literatür uygulamaları (yukarıdaki 82). `/strategies` = Strategy Explorer, community canlı algoritmaları, skor = 1-yıl Sharpe + <1yıl OOS cezası, QC her gün yeniden backtest ediyor, lider tablosu ilk 10'u gösteriyor. **Enumerate edilemedi (JS + hesap kapısı).**\n\n---\n\n## C. KOVA SAYILARI\n\n| Kova | Sayı |\n|---|---|\n| **(a) Bizde ölçüldü / arşiv-örtüşmesi** | **29** |\n| **(b) Yeni kart-adayı** | **8** |\n| **(c) Uygulanamaz** | **45** |\n| *(dizin sayfası, strateji değil)* | *1* |\n| **Toplam yazım** | **83** |\n\n**(a) kovasındaki MEKANİZMA-VARYANTI işaretlileri (bizim ölçmediğimiz açı taşıyanlar):**\n- **24 Liquidity Effect** → EDG-016 (YAŞAYAN) — **yön TERS**: QC/Quantpedia **düşük** turnover'a long, üstelik en küçük-cap çeyreğinde ve **yıllık** rebalansla; bizim yaşayan kenarımız large-cap'te **yüksek** turnover @20. İki bulgu çelişmiyor (evren+ufuk farklı) ama bu, EDG-016'nın \"evren-koşullu mu evrensel mi\" sorusunu doğuruyor. [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/liquidity-effect-in-stocks)\n- **66 Momentum×Volume** → EDG-013/016 — varyant: 3-ay tutuş + 1/3 örtüşük dilim (bizde ölçülen @20 idi)\n- **37 State of Market** → EDG-005 — varyant: SMA yerine 12-ay getiri işareti + kapalı durumda tahvil\n- **162 Momentum in Small Portfolios** → EDG-009 (YAŞAYAN) — **N=10 bizimkiyle birebir aynı**, ama yıllık tutuş (bizde ~63g)\n- **21 Momentum Effect in Stocks** → EDG-009 — varyant: 50 en büyük, 12-ay, aylık, eşit ağırlık\n- **155 Momentum+Reversal+Volatility** → EDG-004/008 — varyant: **vol-quintile-önce koşullu sıralama**\n- **229 Earnings Quality / 1030 G-Score** → EDG-014 ailesi — varyant: **kompozit skor inşası** (bkz. bileşen 5)\n- **01 CAPM Alpha Ranking** → EDG-007 — varyant: 21-günlük CAPM alfası (bizde 12-ay artık momentum)\n- **39 Asset Growth** → EDG-012 komşusu — varyant: hisse ihracı yerine **toplam varlık** büyümesi\n\n---\n\n## D. (b) KOVASI — TAM LİSTE (8 aday)\n\nHepsi: **tez taşır, sayı taşımaz.**\n\n**1. 354 Expected Idiosyncratic Skewness** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/expected-idiosyncratic-skewness)\n- *Tez:* FF3 artıklarından hesaplanan idiyosinkratik vol ve çarpıklığın kesitsel regresyonla ÖNGÖRDÜĞÜ gelecek-ay çarpıklığı, düşük olduğunda large-cap @20 taban-fazlası pozitiftir (Boyer-Mitton-Vorkink: yüksek idio-çarpıklık → düşük beklenen getiri).\n- *Veri:* günlük fiyat + Ken French kütüphanesi FF faktörleri — **FREE ✓**, NLP/analist kilidi yok.\n- *Kill-riski:* **orta**. EDG-004 (MAX) ters yön verdi; bu kart onu ya açıklar ya da vol-ailesinin tümüyle bilgisiz olduğunu teyit eder.\n\n**2. 16 Overnight Anomaly — özellik olarak** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/overnight-anomaly)\n- *Tez:* Günlük getiriyi gecelik (kapanış→açılış) ve gün-içi (açılış→kapanış) bileşenlere ayırdığımızda, gecelik bileşenin 21-günlük toplamı @10–20 taban-fazlası taşır (Lou-Polk-Skouras \"gece ve gündüz\" ayrışması).\n- *Veri:* mevcut OHLC barları — **ek veri SIFIR ✓** (open alanı zaten var).\n- *Kill-riski:* **orta**. Tamamen yeni bir eksen; strateji değil ÖZELLİK olduğu için YAŞAYAN kola doğrudan takılabilir.\n\n**3. 211 PCA-Artığı Kısa-Ufuk Ortalamaya Dönüş** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/mean-reversion-statistical-arbitrage-strategy-in-stocks)\n- *Tez:* Fiyatların 3 ana bileşene (PCA) regresyonundan kalan artığın z-skoru −1,5'in altındayken large-cap @10 taban-fazlası pozitiftir.\n- *Veri:* yalnız fiyat — **FREE ✓**. Long-only uyarlaması doğal (yalnız negatif-z tarafı alınır; RAF'taki koşullu-kısa kısıtına takılmaz).\n- *Kill-riski:* **orta-yüksek**. EDG-007 artık MOMENTUM'u öldürdü ama artık DÖNÜŞÜ (kısa ufuk) ölçülmedi; öte yandan EDG-010 pullback'in ölümü kısa-ufuk dönüş ailesine karşı ön-yargı yaratıyor. QC'nin kendi sayısı %49 maxDD.\n\n**4. 269 Same-Calendar-Month Seasonality** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/seasonality-effect-based-on-same-calendar-month-returns)\n- *Tez:* Bir hissenin geçen yılın AYNI takvim ayındaki getirisi, bu yılın aynı ayı için kesitsel sıralama bilgisi taşır (Keloharju-Linnainmaa-Nyberg, \"Common Factors in Return Seasonalities\").\n- *Veri:* yalnız fiyat, 13 ay geçmiş — **FREE ✓**. @20 ≈ 1 ay, ufkumuzla **uyumlu ✓**.\n- *Kill-riski:* **orta-yüksek**. Bizim takvim ailesi (ToM, EAP, pre-holiday) tümüyle öldü — AMA hepsi ZAMAN-SERİSİ takvimiydi; bu KESİTSEL mevsimsellik, farklı eksen. QC'nin kendi Sharpe'ı 0,128 (SPY 0,773).\n\n**5. 125 12-Month Cycle in Cross-Section** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/12-month-cycle-in-cross-section-of-stocks-returns)\n- *Tez:* 4'ün özel hali — geçen yılın Ocak ayı performansı bu yılın Ocak'ını öngörür (t−365 ile t−335 gün arası getiri).\n- *Veri:* yalnız fiyat — **FREE ✓**.\n- *Kill-riski:* **yüksek**. 4 numaranın dar bir alt-kümesi; 4 ölçülürse bu ayrıca ölçülmemeli (K israfı). **4 ile aynı kartta bir grid hücresi olarak koşulmalı.**\n\n**6. 77 Beta Factors in Stocks** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/beta-factors-in-stocks)\n- *Tez:* SPY'ye karşı düşük-beta hisseler large-cap @20 taban-fazlası taşır (düşük-beta anomalisi).\n- *Veri:* fiyat + SPY — **FREE ✓**.\n- *Kill-riski:* **yüksek**. Vol ailesi bizde neredeyse tümüyle arşiv; dahası EDG-004'ün ters-yön bulgusu (yüksek-MAX daha iyi) düşük-beta tezinin TERSİNİ ima ediyor. Değeri edge olarak değil, **tutarlılık sınavı** olarak.\n\n**7. 102 Option Expiration Week Effect** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/option-expiration-week-effect)\n- *Tez:* Opsiyon vade haftasında (ayın 3. Cuma'sını içeren hafta) large-cap taban-fazlası sistematik olarak farklıdır.\n- *Veri:* takvim + fiyat — **FREE ✓** (opsiyon verisi GEREKMEZ, yalnız takvim).\n- *Kill-riski:* **çok yüksek**. Takvim ailesi bizde öldü (ToM kill#2 ön-adımda tetiklendi). Yalnız tamlık için listede.\n\n**8. 14 Sektör-Göreli Momentum (uyarlanmış)** — [URL](https://www.quantconnect.com/tutorials/strategy-library/sector-momentum)\n- *Tez:* Hissenin ham momentumundan kendi sektörünün momentumu çıkarıldığında kalan sektör-göreli momentum, ham momentumun üstünde taban-fazlası taşır.\n- *Veri:* fiyat + sektör sınıflaması — **PIT ŞERHİ**: sektör ataması as-of değilse YASAK (PIT'siz fundamentals proxy yasağı).\n- *Kill-riski:* **çok yüksek**. EDG-007'nin kill gerekçesi tam olarak \"rawmom-örtüşmesi\" idi; sektör-demeaning büyük olasılıkla aynı kill'e çarpar.\n\n---\n\n## E. ÖNCELİK SIRALI İLK 5 — ve bir uyarı\n\n> **Uyarı:** (b) kovasının tamamının beklenen değeri, **A bölümündeki bileşen derslerinin altında.** Sekiz adayın hiçbiri YAŞAYAN kola dokunmuyor; A(1) ratchet-olmayan çıkış, A(2) korelasyon-çarpanı ve A(6) sürekli-rejim kartları ise doğrudan **mevcut, canlı, para kazanan** kolun üzerinde çalışıyor. K bütçesi kısıtlıysa sıra A'dan başlamalı.\n\n(b) içinde sıralama:\n\n1. **354 Expected Idiosyncratic Skewness** — tek gerekçe: arşivdeki bir sonucu (EDG-004 ters-yön MAX) **açıklama** potansiyeli taşıyan tek aday; yeni edge bulmasa bile bilgi üretir. Veri ücretsiz, mekanizma net.\n2. **16 Overnight decomposition** — en ucuz gerçek yenilik: sıfır ek veri, sıfır PIT riski, tamamen ölçülmemiş eksen, ve strateji değil ÖZELLİK olduğu için YAŞAYAN kola takılabilir (ayrı motor gerektirmez).\n3. **269 (+125 tek kartta)** — kesitsel mevsimsellik, bizim ölü takvim ailesinden yapısal olarak farklı eksen; @20 ufkumuza birebir oturuyor; 125 ayrı kart değil grid hücresi olmalı.\n4. **211 PCA-artığı dönüş** — mekanizma temiz ve veri bedava, ama EDG-010'un ölümü kısa-ufuk dönüş ailesine karşı ağır ön-yargı bırakıyor; ancak 1–3 sonuçsuz kalırsa sıraya girmeli.\n5. **77 Beta** — edge beklentisiyle değil, EDG-004'ün ters-yön bulgusunun **bağımsız doğrulaması** olarak; tek başına açılmamalı, 354 ile aynı kartta ikinci hücre olarak koşulmalı.\n\n**Listelenmeyen 102 ve 14:** ön-yargı o kadar ağır ki tek başına K harcamaya değmez.\n\n---\n\n## Kapanmamış kapı + bir yan gözlem\n\n- **Asıl kazanan madeni taranmadı.** Strategy Library literatür deposu ve QC'nin kendi sayılarıyla çoğunlukla SPY-altı. Gerçek \"kazanan/olgun\" küme Strategy Explorer'da ve enumerate edilemedi. Operatörün hesabı 2026-08-03'te açıldığına göre (EDG-021), **Explorer envanteri ayrı bir tur olarak koşulabilir** — orada bileşen dersleri hem daha güncel hem de OOS-cezalı bir skorla filtrelenmiş olur.\n- **Yan gözlem (görev dışı, K-defteri bütünlüğü):** `research/cards/` dizininde **EDG-2026-019 yok** — 018'den 020'ye atlıyor.
